\displaystyle I_{4}=\sum_{i=2}^{2n_{0}+1}\lambda_{i}^{(t)}\mathbf{v}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{(t)}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{(t)\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)}.

We give upper bounds of
|I_{1}|
,
|I_{2}|
,
|I_{3}|
and
|I_{4}|
. By the induction hypothesis that
\mathbf{v}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)}\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle\geq(1-\sigma_{0})\cdot\lambda_{1}\cdot(1-4\sigma_{0}^{2})^{2}\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle-\lambda_{1}\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq(1-2\sigma_{0})\cdot\lambda_{1}\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle-\lambda_{1}\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq-2\sigma_{0}\lambda_{1}\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle,

where the first inequality follows by Lemma
B.4
.
Similarly, we also

\displaystyle I_{1}\leq(1+\sigma_{0})\cdot\lambda_{1}\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle-\lambda_{1}\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle\leq\sigma_{0}\lambda_{1}\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle.

Therefore, we conclude that

\displaystyle|I_{1}|\leq 2\sigma_{0}\lambda_{1}\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle.

(B.25)

Let
\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}=\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{v}_{1}\mathbf{v}_{1}^{\top}
. Then for
I_{2}
, by the property of project matrices, we have
\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}=\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}
, and therefore

\displaystyle|I_{2}|

\displaystyle=|\lambda_{1}^{(t)}\mathbf{v}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{v}_{1}^{(t)}(\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{v}_{1}^{(t)})^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)}|

\displaystyle\leq(1+\sigma_{0})\lambda_{1}\cdot 4\sigma_{0}\cdot\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq 8\sigma_{0}\lambda_{1}\cdot\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq 8\sqrt{\sigma_{0}}\cdot\lambda_{1}\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle,

(B.26)

where the first inequality follows by Lemma
B.4
, and the third inequality follows by the induction hypothesis.
For
I_{3}
, we have

\displaystyle|I_{3}|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=2}^{2n_{0}+1}|\lambda_{i}^{(t)}|\cdot|\mathbf{v}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{(t)}|^{2}\cdot\mathbf{v}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\leq 2n_{0}\cdot\bigg(\frac{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}{2(1-\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}+\sigma_{0}\bigg)\cdot\lambda_{1}\cdot 16\sigma_{0}^{2}\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq n_{0}\sigma_{0}^{2}\lambda_{1}\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle,

(B.27)

where in the second inequality we use Lemmas
5.2
and
B.4
, and the last inequality follows by the assumption that
\sigma_{0},\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}\leq 1/64
.
Finally for
I_{4}
, we have

\displaystyle|I_{4}|

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg\|\sum_{i=2}^{2n_{0}+1}\lambda_{i}^{(t)}\cdot\langle\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{i}^{(t)}\rangle\cdot\mathbf{v}_{i}^{(t)\top}\Bigg\|_{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\sum_{i=2}^{2n_{0}+1}\lambda_{i}^{(t)2}\cdot\langle\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{i}^{(t)}\rangle^{2}}\cdot\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2n_{0}}\cdot\bigg(\frac{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}{2(1-\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}+\sigma_{0}\bigg)\cdot\lambda_{1}\cdot 4\sigma_{0}\cdot\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{n_{0}}\cdot\sigma_{0}\lambda_{1}\cdot\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{n_{0}\sigma_{0}}\cdot\lambda_{1}\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle,

(B.28)

where the second inequality follows by the fact that
\mathbf{v}_{i}^{(t)}
,
i=2,\ldots,2n_{0}+1
are mutually orthogonal unit vectors, the third inequality follows by Lemmas
5.2
and
B.4
, the fourth inequality follows by the assumption that
\sigma_{0},\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}\leq 1/64
, and the fifth inequality follows by the induction hypothesis.

Now plugging (
B.25
), (
B.26
), (
B.27
) and (
B.28
) into (
B.24
), we obtain

\displaystyle[1+(1-2\sqrt{n_{0}\sigma_{0}})\lambda_{1}]\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle\leq\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t+1)},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle\leq[1+(1+2\sqrt{n_{0}\sigma_{0}})\lambda_{1}]\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle

(B.29)

for all
t=0,\ldots,t_{0}
and
r\in\mathcal{I}^{+}
,
where we use the assumption that
\sigma_{0}\leq 1/(8n_{0}^{2})
and
\sigma_{0}\leq 1/512
.

Moreover, for
t=0,\ldots,t_{0}
and
r\in[2m]
, we also have

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t+1)}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)}+\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}(\mathbf{A}+\bm{\Xi}^{(t)})\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)}+\lambda_{1}^{(t)}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{v}_{1}^{(t)}\mathbf{v}_{1}^{(t)\top}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)}+\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\Bigg(\sum_{i=2}^{2n_{0}+1}\lambda_{i}^{(t)}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{(t)}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{(t)\top}\Bigg)\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)}+I_{5}+I_{6}+I_{7}+I_{8},

(B.30)

where

\displaystyle I_{5}=\lambda_{1}^{(t)}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{v}_{1}^{(t)}\mathbf{v}_{1}^{(t)\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)},

\displaystyle I_{6}=\lambda_{1}^{(t)}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{v}_{1}^{(t)}\mathbf{v}_{1}^{(t)\top}\mathbf{v}_{1}\mathbf{v}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)},

\displaystyle I_{7}=\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\Bigg(\sum_{i=2}^{2n_{0}+1}\lambda_{i}^{(t)}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{(t)}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{(t)\top}\Bigg)\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)},

\displaystyle I_{8}=\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\Bigg(\sum_{i=2}^{2n_{0}+1}\lambda_{i}^{(t)}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{(t)}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{(t)\top}\Bigg)\mathbf{v}_{1}\mathbf{v}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)}.

For
I_{5}
, we have

\displaystyle|I_{5}|

\displaystyle\leq(1+\sigma_{0})\lambda_{1}\cdot 16\sigma_{0}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq 32\lambda_{1}\sigma_{0}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)}\|_{2},

(B.31)

where the first inequality follows by Lemma
B.4
. For
I_{6}
, we have

\displaystyle|I_{6}|

\displaystyle\leq(1+\sigma_{0})\lambda_{1}\cdot 4\sigma_{0}\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq 8\lambda_{1}\sigma_{0}\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq 8\lambda_{1}\sqrt{\sigma_{0}}\cdot\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)}\|_{2},

(B.32)

where the first inequality follows by Lemma
B.4
, and the third inequality follows by the induction hypothesis. For
I_{7}
, we have

\displaystyle|I_{7}|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i\geq 2}|\lambda_{i}^{(t)}|\cdot\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\frac{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}{2(1-\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}+\sigma_{0}\bigg)\cdot\lambda_{1}\cdot\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{3}\cdot\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}\cdot\lambda_{1}\cdot\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)}\|_{2},

(B.33)

where the second inequality follows by Lemmas
5.2
and
B.4
, and the third inequality follows by the assumption that
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}\leq 1/16
and
\sigma_{0}\leq\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}/16
. For
I_{8}
, we have

\displaystyle|I_{8}|

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg\|\sum_{i=2}^{2n_{0}+1}\lambda_{i}^{(t)}\cdot\langle\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{i}^{(t)}\rangle\cdot\mathbf{v}_{i}^{(t)}\Bigg\|_{2}\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\sum_{i=2}^{2n_{0}+1}\lambda_{i}^{(t)2}\cdot\langle\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{i}^{(t)}\rangle^{2}}\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2n_{0}}\cdot\bigg(\frac{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}{2(1-\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}+\sigma_{0}\bigg)\cdot\lambda_{1}\cdot 4\sigma_{0}\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{n_{0}}\cdot\lambda_{1}\sigma_{0}\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{n_{0}\sigma_{0}}\cdot\lambda_{1}\cdot\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)}\|_{2},

(B.34)

where the second inequality follows by the fact that
\mathbf{v}_{i}^{(t)}
,
i=2,\ldots,2n_{0}+1
are mutually orthogonal unit vectors, the third inequality follows by Lemmas
5.2
and
B.4
, the fourth inequality follows by the assumption that
\sigma_{0},\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}\leq 1/64
, and the fifth inequality follows by the induction hypothesis. Now combining (
B.30
), (
B.31
), (
B.32
), (
B.33
), and (
B.34
) gives

\displaystyle\bigg(1-\frac{5}{6}\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}\lambda_{1}\bigg)\cdot\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)}\|_{2}\leq\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t+1)}\|_{2}\leq\bigg(1+\frac{5}{6}\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}\lambda_{1}\bigg)\cdot\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)}\|_{2}

(B.35)

for all
t=0,\ldots,t_{0}
and
r\in[2m]
, where we use the assumption that
\sigma_{0}\leq\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}^{2}/(64n_{0})
and
\sigma_{0}\leq\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}/16
.

Now by (
B.29
) and (
B.35
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t_{0}+1)},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle}{\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t_{0}+1)}\|_{2}}\geq\frac{1+(1-2\sqrt{n_{0}\sigma_{0}})\lambda_{1}}{1+(5/6)\cdot\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}\cdot\lambda_{1}}\cdot\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t_{0})},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle}{\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t_{0})}\|_{2}}\geq\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t_{0})},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle}{\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t_{0})}\|_{2}}\geq\sigma_{0},

for all
r\in\mathcal{I}^{+}
, where the second inequality follows by the assumption that
\sigma_{0}\leq\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}^{2}/(64n_{0})
.
This verifies the induction hypothesis (
B.22
) at
t=t_{0}+1
. Moreover, by (
B.29
) and (
B.35
), we also have

\displaystyle\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t_{0}+1)},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle}{\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t_{0}+1)}\|_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X}}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t_{0}+1)}\|_{2}}{\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t_{0}+1)}\|_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{[1+(1+\sigma_{0})\lambda_{1}]\cdot\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X}}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t_{0})}\|_{2}}{(1-5\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}\lambda_{1}/6)\cdot\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t_{0})}\|_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq[1+(1+\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})\lambda_{1}]\cdot\frac{\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X}}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t_{0})}\|_{2}}{\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t_{0})}\|_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq[1+(1+\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})\lambda_{1}]^{t_{0}}\cdot\frac{\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X}}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(0)}\|_{2}}{\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(0)}\|_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq[1+(1-\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})\lambda_{1}]^{\frac{1+\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}{1-\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}\cdot t_{0}}\cdot\frac{\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X}}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(0)}\|_{2}}{\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(0)}\|_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq[1+(1+\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})\lambda_{1}]^{2T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}\cdot\frac{\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X}}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(0)}\|_{2}}{\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(0)}\|_{2}}

for all
r\in[2m]
, where the second inequality follows by Lemma
B.4
, the third inequality follows by the assumption that
\sigma_{0}\leq\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}/64
, and the fifth inequality follows by the fact that
1+a\leq(1+b)^{a/b}
for all
a>b>0
.
Now by the definition of
T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}
, we know that

\displaystyle[1+(1-\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})\lambda_{1}]^{T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}\leq\max\Bigg\{288M^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\cdot\frac{\log(2/\sigma_{0})^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\cdot\sqrt{\log(dn)}\cdot\sqrt{\log(md)}}{n^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\mathrm{SNR}^{\frac{q}{q-2}}},2\Bigg\}.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t_{0}+1)},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle}{\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t_{0}+1)}\|_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\max\Bigg\{288M^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\cdot\frac{\log(2/\sigma_{0})^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\cdot\sqrt{\log(dn)}\cdot\sqrt{\log(md)}}{n^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\mathrm{SNR}^{\frac{q}{q-2}}},2\Bigg\}\cdot\frac{\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X}}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(0)}\|_{2}}{\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(0)}\|_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\max\Bigg\{288M^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\cdot\frac{\log(2/\sigma_{0})^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\cdot\sqrt{\log(dn)}\cdot\sqrt{\log(md)}}{n^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\mathrm{SNR}^{\frac{q}{q-2}}},2\Bigg\}\cdot\frac{4\sigma_{0}\cdot\sqrt{n_{0}}}{\sigma_{0}\cdot\sqrt{n_{0}}}

\displaystyle\leq\sigma_{0}^{-1}

for all
r\in[2m]
,
where the second inequality follows by (
B.19
) and (
B.20
), and the last inequality follows by the assumption that
\sigma_{0}\leq\widetilde{O}(M^{-\frac{1}{q-2}}\cdot n^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\mathrm{SNR}^{\frac{q}{q-2}})
. This verifies the induction hypothesis (
B.23
)
at
t=t_{0}+1
.

Based on the discussion above, by induction, we conclude that (
B.21
), (
B.22
), (
B.23
), (
B.29
), and (
B.35
) hold for all
t=0,\ldots,T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}
. In other words, we can conclude that:

\displaystyle[1+(1-2\sqrt{n_{0}\sigma_{0}})\lambda_{1}]\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle\leq\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t+1)},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle\leq[1+(1+2\sqrt{n_{0}\sigma_{0}})\lambda_{1}]\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle

(B.36)

for all
t=0,\ldots,T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}
and
r\in\mathcal{I}^{+}
, and

\displaystyle\bigg(1-\frac{5}{6}\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}\lambda_{1}\bigg)\cdot\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)}\|_{2}\leq\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t+1)}\|_{2}\leq\bigg(1+\frac{5}{6}\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}\lambda_{1}\bigg)\cdot\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)}\|_{2}

(B.37)

for all
t=0,\ldots,T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}
and
r\in[2m]
. Moreover, by Lemma
5.1
and the fact that the columns and rows of
\mathbf{A}
,
\bm{\Xi}^{(t)}
are in
\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{col}}
and
\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{row}}
respectively, we also have

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X}}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t+1)}=[\mathbf{I}+\mathbf{A}+\bm{\Xi}^{(t)}]\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X}}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)}

for all
t=0,\ldots,T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}
and all
r\in[2m]
. Therefore, by Lemmas
5.2
and
B.4
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X}}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t+1)}\|_{2}\leq(1+(1+\sigma_{0})\lambda_{1})\cdot\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X}}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)}\|_{2}

(B.38)

for all
t=0,\ldots,T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}
and all
r\in[2m]
.
By (
B.18
) and (
B.36
), we have

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq[1+(1-2\sqrt{n_{0}\sigma_{0}})\lambda_{1}]^{T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(0)},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq[1+(1-2\sqrt{n_{0}\sigma_{0}})\lambda_{1}]^{T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}\cdot\sigma_{0}/2

(B.39)

for all
r\in\mathcal{I}^{+}
.
Moreover, by (
B.19
) and (
B.37
), we also have

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(1+\frac{5}{6}\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}\lambda_{1}\bigg)^{T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}\cdot\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(1+\frac{5}{6}\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}\lambda_{1}\bigg)^{T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}\cdot 2\sigma_{0}\cdot\sqrt{n_{0}}

(B.40)

for all
r\in[2m]
. In addition, by (
B.38
), it holds that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X}}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{2}\leq\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X}}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(0)}\|_{2}\leq 4(1+(1+\sigma_{0})\lambda_{1})^{T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}\cdot\sigma_{0}\cdot\sqrt{n_{0}}

(B.41)

for all
t=0,\ldots,T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}
and all
r\in[2m]
, where the last inequality above is by (
B.20
).

Now denote
\bar{\bm{\mu}}=\bm{\mu}/\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}
, and
\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\mu}}^{\perp}=\mathbf{I}-\bar{\bm{\mu}}\bar{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}
.
Then for all
r\in\mathcal{I}^{+}
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})},\bar{\bm{\mu}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})},\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X}}\bar{\bm{\mu}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})},(\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}+\mathbf{v}_{1}\mathbf{v}_{1}^{\top})\bar{\bm{\mu}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})},(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X}})\bar{\bm{\mu}}\rangle+\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})},\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\bar{\bm{\mu}}\rangle+\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle\cdot\langle\mathbf{v}_{1},\bar{\bm{\mu}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})},\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\bar{\bm{\mu}}\rangle+\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle\cdot\langle\mathbf{v}_{1},\bar{\bm{\mu}}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq-\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{2}+\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle\cdot(1-\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}),

(B.42)

where the first inequality follows by Lemma
5.2
.
Moreover, we also have

\displaystyle\frac{[1+(1-2\sqrt{n_{0}\sigma_{0}})\lambda_{1}]^{T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}\cdot\sigma_{0}/2}{2\sigma_{0}\sqrt{n_{0}}\cdot(1+5\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}\lambda_{1}/6)^{T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{4\sqrt{n_{0}}}\cdot[1+(1-\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})\cdot\lambda_{1}]^{T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{4\sqrt{n_{0}}}\cdot\max\Bigg\{288M^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\cdot\frac{\log(2/\sigma_{0})^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\cdot\sqrt{\log(dn)}\cdot\sqrt{\log(md)}}{n^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\mathrm{SNR}^{\frac{q}{q-2}}},2\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{4\sigma_{0}\sqrt{n_{0}}}

\displaystyle\geq 2,

where the third inequality is by the
\sigma_{0}\leq\widetilde{O}(M^{-\frac{1}{q-2}}\cdot n^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\mathrm{SNR}^{\frac{q}{q-2}})
, and the last inequality is by
\sigma_{0}\leq 1/(64n_{0})
.
Therefore, by (
B.39
) and (
B.40
), we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{2}\leq\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle/4,

and hence by (
B.42
), we have

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})},\bm{\mu}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})},\bar{\bm{\mu}}\rangle\cdot\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\geq\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle\cdot\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}/4

\displaystyle\geq[1+(1-2\sqrt{n_{0}\sigma_{0}})\lambda_{1}]^{T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}\cdot\sigma_{0}\cdot\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}/8,

(B.43)

for all
r\in\mathcal{I}^{+}
, where the last inequality follows by (
B.39
).
In addition, since the update only happens in the subspace
\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{col}}
which is spanned by the data, we have
(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X}})\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}=(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X}})\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(0)}
.
Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\mu}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X}}+\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}+\mathbf{v}_{1}\mathbf{v}_{1}^{\top}-\bar{\bm{\mu}}\bar{\bm{\mu}}^{\top})\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X}})\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{2}+\|(\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}+\mathbf{v}_{1}\mathbf{v}_{1}^{\top}-\bar{\bm{\mu}}\bar{\bm{\mu}}^{\top})\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X}})\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(0)}\|_{2}+\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{2}+\|(\mathbf{v}_{1}\mathbf{v}_{1}^{\top}-\bar{\bm{\mu}}\bar{\bm{\mu}}^{\top})\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{2},

(B.44)

where the last inequality follows by the fact that
(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X}})\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(0)}
is a centered spherical Gaussian random vector with standard deviation bounded by
\sigma_{0}
, and by Gaussian tail bound, with probability at least
1-d^{-2}
,

\displaystyle\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X}})\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(0)}\|_{3}\leq 4\sigma_{0}\cdot\sqrt{d\log(md)},

(B.45)

for all
r\in[2m]
.
Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\|(\mathbf{v}_{1}\mathbf{v}_{1}^{\top}-\bar{\bm{\mu}}\bar{\bm{\mu}}^{\top})\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|(\mathbf{v}_{1}-\bar{\bm{\mu}})\mathbf{v}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{2}+\|\bar{\bm{\mu}}(\mathbf{v}_{1}-\bar{\bm{\mu}})^{\top}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle\cdot\|\mathbf{v}_{1}-\bar{\bm{\mu}}\|_{2}+|(\mathbf{v}_{1}-\bar{\bm{\mu}})^{\top}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}|

\displaystyle\leq\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle\cdot\|\mathbf{v}_{1}-\bar{\bm{\mu}}\|_{2}+|(\mathbf{v}_{1}-\bar{\bm{\mu}})^{\top}\mathbf{v}_{1}\cdot\mathbf{v}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}|

\displaystyle\quad+|(\mathbf{v}_{1}-\bar{\bm{\mu}})^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}|

\displaystyle\leq\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle\cdot\|\mathbf{v}_{1}-\bar{\bm{\mu}}\|_{2}+\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle\cdot|(\mathbf{v}_{1}-\bar{\bm{\mu}})^{\top}\mathbf{v}_{1}|

\displaystyle\quad+\|\mathbf{v}_{1}-\bar{\bm{\mu}}\|_{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq(\sqrt{2}\cdot\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}+\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}^{2})\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\sqrt{2}\cdot\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}\cdot\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle+\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}\cdot\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X}}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{2}+\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{2}.

(B.46)

Plugging (
B.46
) and (
B.45
) into (
B.44
) gives

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\mu}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{2}\leq 4\sigma_{0}\cdot\sqrt{d\log(md)}+2\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}\cdot\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X}}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{2}+2\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{2}.

Therefore, we have that for
r\in\mathcal{I}^{+}
and
r^{\prime}\in[2m]
,

\displaystyle\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})},\bm{\mu}\rangle}{\|\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\mu}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r^{\prime}}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})},\bm{\mu}\rangle}{4\sigma_{0}\cdot\sqrt{d\log(md)}+2\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}\cdot\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X}}\mathbf{w}_{r^{\prime}}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{2}+2\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r^{\prime}}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{3}\cdot\min\Bigg\{\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})},\bm{\mu}\rangle}{4\sigma_{0}\cdot\sqrt{d\log(md)}},\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})},\bm{\mu}\rangle}{2\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}\cdot\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X}}\mathbf{w}_{r^{\prime}}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{2}},\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})},\bm{\mu}\rangle}{2\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r^{\prime}}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{2}}\Bigg\}

By (
B.39
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})},\bm{\mu}\rangle}{4\sigma_{0}\cdot\sqrt{d\log(md)}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{[1+(1-2\sqrt{n_{0}\sigma_{0}})\lambda_{1}]^{T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}\cdot\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}}{4\sqrt{d\log(md)}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{[1+(1-\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})\lambda_{1}]^{T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}\cdot\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}\cdot\sigma_{p}}{4\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d\log(md)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{[1+(1-\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})\lambda_{1}]^{T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}\cdot\mathrm{SNR}\cdot\sigma_{p}}{4\sqrt{\log(md)}}

\displaystyle\geq 72C^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\sigma_{p}\cdot\frac{\log(2/\sigma_{0})^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\cdot\sqrt{\log(dn)}}{n^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\mathrm{SNR}^{\frac{2}{q-2}}},

where the first inequality is by the assumption that
\sigma_{0}\leq\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}/(4n_{0})
, and the second inequality is by the definition of
T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}
, which implies that

\displaystyle[1+(1-\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})\lambda_{1}]^{T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}\geq 288M^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\cdot\frac{\log(2/\sigma_{0})^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\cdot\sqrt{\log(dn)}\cdot\sqrt{\log(md)}}{n^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\mathrm{SNR}^{\frac{q}{q-2}}}.

By (
B.41
) and (
B.43
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})},\bm{\mu}\rangle}{2\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}\cdot\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X}}\mathbf{w}_{r^{\prime}}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{[1+(1-2\sqrt{n_{0}\sigma_{0}})\lambda_{1}]^{T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}\cdot\sigma_{0}\cdot\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}/8}{8\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}\cdot(1+(1+\sigma_{0})\lambda_{1})^{T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}\cdot\sigma_{0}\cdot\sqrt{n_{0}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{[1+(1-2\sqrt{n_{0}\sigma_{0}})\lambda_{1}]^{T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}\cdot\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}}{64\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}\cdot(1+(1+\sigma_{0})\lambda_{1})^{T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}\cdot\sqrt{n_{0}}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}}{64\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}\cdot(1+(1+\sigma_{0})\lambda_{1})^{T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}\cdot\sqrt{n_{0}}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}}{64\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}\cdot(1+(1-\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})\lambda_{1})^{\frac{1+\sigma_{0}}{1-\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}\cdot T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}\cdot\sqrt{n_{0}}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}}{64\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}\cdot(1+(1-\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})\lambda_{1})^{2T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}\cdot\sqrt{n_{0}}},

where the second inequality follows by the assumption that
\sigma_{0}\leq 1/(4n_{0})
, the third inequality follows by the fact that
1+a\leq(1+b)^{a/b}
for all
a>b>0
, and the fourth inequality follows by the assumption that
\sigma_{0},\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}\leq 1/4
. Now by the definition of
T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}
, we know that

\displaystyle[1+(1-\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})\lambda_{1}]^{T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}\leq\max\Bigg\{288M^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\cdot\frac{\log(2/\sigma_{0})^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\cdot\sqrt{\log(dn)}\cdot\sqrt{\log(md)}}{n^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\mathrm{SNR}^{\frac{q}{q-2}}},2\Bigg\}.

Therefore, by the assumption that

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}\leq\frac{\sqrt{d}\cdot n^{\frac{3}{q-2}}\mathrm{SNR}^{\frac{3q}{q-2}}}{64\cdot 288^{2}\cdot 72\cdot\sqrt{n_{0}}\cdot M^{\frac{3}{q-2}}\cdot\log(2/\sigma_{0})^{\frac{3}{q-2}}\cdot\log(dn)^{\frac{3}{2}}\cdot\log(md)},

we have

\displaystyle\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})},\bm{\mu}\rangle}{2\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}\cdot\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X}}\mathbf{w}_{r^{\prime}}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}}{64\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}(1+(1-\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})\lambda_{1})^{2T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}\sqrt{n_{0}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathrm{SNR}\cdot\sigma_{p}\cdot\sqrt{d}}{64\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}(1+(1-\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})\lambda_{1})^{2T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}\sqrt{n_{0}}}

\displaystyle\geq 72M^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\sigma_{p}\cdot\frac{\log(2/\sigma_{0})^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\cdot\sqrt{\log(dn)}}{n^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\mathrm{SNR}^{\frac{2}{q-2}}}.

Finally,
by (
B.39
) and (
B.40
), we also have

\displaystyle\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})},\bm{\mu}\rangle}{2\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathcal{X},\mathbf{v}_{1}}^{\perp}\mathbf{w}_{r^{\prime}}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{[1+(1-2\sqrt{n_{0}\sigma_{0}})\lambda_{1}]^{T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}\cdot\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}}{8\sqrt{n_{0}}\cdot\big(1+\frac{5}{6}\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}\lambda_{1}\big)^{T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{[1+(1-\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})\lambda_{1}]^{T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}\cdot\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}}{8\sqrt{n_{0}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{[1+(1-\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})\lambda_{1}]^{T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}\cdot\mathrm{SNR}\cdot\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d}}{8\sqrt{n_{0}}}

\displaystyle\geq 72M^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\sigma_{p}\cdot\frac{\log(2/\sigma_{0})^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\cdot\sqrt{\log(dn)}}{n^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\mathrm{SNR}^{\frac{2}{q-2}}},

where the second inequality follows by the assumption that
\sigma_{0}\leq\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}^{2}/(64n_{0})
, and the last inequality follows by the choice of
T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}
which implies that

\displaystyle[1+(1-\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})\lambda_{1}]^{T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}\cdot}{}

\displaystyle\geq 288M^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\cdot\frac{\log(2/\sigma_{0})^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\cdot\sqrt{\log(dn)}\cdot\sqrt{\log(md)}}{n^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\mathrm{SNR}^{\frac{q}{q-2}}}

\displaystyle\geq 576M^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{n_{0}}\cdot\log(2/\sigma_{0})^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\cdot\sqrt{\log(dn)}}{n^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\mathrm{SNR}^{\frac{q}{q-2}}\cdot\sqrt{d}},

where the second inequality follows by the assumption that
d\geq 4n_{0}
.
Therefore, we conclude that for
r\in\mathcal{I}^{+}
and
r^{\prime}\in[2m]
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})},\bm{\mu}\rangle}{\|(\mathbf{I}-\bm{\mu}\bm{\mu}^{\top}/\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2})\mathbf{w}_{r^{\prime}}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{2}}\geq 24M^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\sigma_{p}\cdot\frac{\log(2/\sigma_{0})^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\cdot\sqrt{\log(dn)}}{n^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\mathrm{SNR}^{\frac{2}{q-2}}},

(B.47)

Now for any
{r^{\prime}}\in[2m]
, consider the following decomposition for
\mathbf{w}_{r^{\prime}}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}
:

\displaystyle\mathbf{w}_{r^{\prime}}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}=\mathbf{w}_{r^{\prime}}^{\perp}+\gamma_{r^{\prime}}\cdot\bm{\mu}/\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\rho_{{r^{\prime}},i}\cdot\bm{\xi}_{i}^{\text{fine-tuning}}/\|\bm{\xi}_{i}^{\text{fine-tuning}}\|_{2}^{2},

where
\mathbf{w}_{r^{\prime}}^{\perp}
is perpendicular to
\bm{\mu}
and
\bm{\xi}_{1}^{\text{fine-tuning}},\ldots,\bm{\xi}_{n}^{\text{fine-tuning}}
.
Then we directly have

\displaystyle\gamma_{r}=\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})},\bm{\mu}\rangle

(B.48)

for all
r\in\mathcal{I}^{+}
.
Note that
\mathbf{w}_{r^{\prime}}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}
is independent of
\bm{\xi}_{i}^{\text{fine-tuning}}
,
i\in[n]
.
For any
i\in[n]
, considering the randomness of
\bm{\xi}_{i}^{\text{fine-tuning}}
, we see that
\langle\mathbf{w}_{r^{\prime}}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})},\bm{\xi}_{i}^{\text{fine-tuning}}\rangle
is a Gaussian random variable with mean zero and standard deviation
\sigma_{p}\cdot\|\mathbf{w}_{r^{\prime}}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{2}
. Therefore, by Gaussian tail bound and union bound, with probability at least
1-d^{-2}
, we have

\displaystyle|\langle\mathbf{w}_{r^{\prime}}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})},\bm{\xi}_{i}^{\text{fine-tuning}}\rangle|\leq 8\sigma_{p}\cdot\|\mathbf{w}_{r^{\prime}}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{2}\cdot\sqrt{\log(dn)}.

Now denote
\mathbf{E}=[\bm{\xi}_{1},\ldots,\bm{\xi}_{n}]
,
\mathbf{D}={\rm diag}(\|\bm{\xi}_{1}^{\text{fine-tuning}}\|_{2}^{-2},\|\bm{\xi}_{2}^{\text{fine-tuning}}\|_{2}^{-2},\ldots,\|\bm{\xi}_{n}^{\text{fine-tuning}}\|_{2}^{-2})
,
\bm{\rho}_{r^{\prime}}=[\rho_{r^{\prime},1},\ldots,\rho_{{r^{\prime}},n}]^{\top}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{w}_{r^{\prime}}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})\top}\mathbf{E}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\|\mathbf{w}_{r^{\prime}}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})\top}(\mathbf{I}-\bm{\mu}\bm{\mu}^{\top}/\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2})\mathbf{E}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq 8\sigma_{p}\cdot\|(\mathbf{I}-\bm{\mu}\bm{\mu}^{\top}/\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2})\mathbf{w}_{r^{\prime}}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{2}\cdot\sqrt{\log(dn)}.

By definition, we have

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{r^{\prime}}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})},\bm{\xi}_{i_{0}}\rangle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\rho_{{r^{\prime}},i}\cdot\langle\bm{\xi}_{i},\bm{\xi}_{i_{0}}\rangle/\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{2},

and

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\mathbf{w}_{r^{\prime}}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}=\mathbf{E}^{\top}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{D}\bm{\rho}_{r^{\prime}}.

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\big|[(\mathbf{E}^{\top}\mathbf{E})^{-1}]_{ij}\big|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma_{p}^{2}d}\cdot\big|[(\mathbf{I}+\sigma_{p}^{-2}d^{-1}\cdot\mathbf{E}^{\top}\mathbf{E}-\mathbf{I}))^{-1}]_{ij}\big|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma_{p}^{2}d}\cdot\Bigg|\Bigg[\mathbf{I}+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(\mathbf{I}-\sigma_{p}^{-2}d^{-1}\cdot\mathbf{E}^{\top}\mathbf{E})^{k}\Bigg]_{ij}\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}3/(2\sigma_{p}^{2}d),&\text{if }i=j,\\
1/(2n\sigma_{p}^{2}d),&\text{if }i\neq j,\end{cases}

(B.49)

where the last inequality follows by the fact that
(\mathbf{I}-\sigma_{p}^{-2}d^{-1}\cdot\mathbf{E}^{\top}\mathbf{E})^{k}
is an
n\times n
matrix whose entries are bounded by
\widetilde{O}(d^{-1/2})
according to Lemma
C.8
.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\|\bm{\rho}_{r^{\prime}}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{D}^{-1}(\mathbf{E}^{\top}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{w}_{r^{\prime}}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{D}^{-1}\|_{\infty}\cdot\|(\mathbf{E}^{\top}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\|_{\infty}\cdot\|\mathbf{E}\mathbf{w}_{r^{\prime}}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3}{2}\sigma_{p}^{2}d\cdot\frac{2}{\sigma_{p}^{2}d}\cdot 8\sigma_{p}\cdot\|(\mathbf{I}-\bm{\mu}\bm{\mu}^{\top}/\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2})\mathbf{w}_{r^{\prime}}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{2}\cdot\sqrt{\log(dn)}

\displaystyle=24\sigma_{p}\cdot\|(\mathbf{I}-\bm{\mu}\bm{\mu}^{\top}/\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2})\mathbf{w}_{r^{\prime}}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{2}\cdot\sqrt{\log(dn)}

(B.50)

for all
{r^{\prime}}\in[2m]
,
where the third inequality follows by (
B.49
) and Lemma
C.8
. Therefore, combining (
B.47
), (
B.48
) and (
B.50
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma_{r}}{|\rho_{{r^{\prime}},i}|}\geq\frac{\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})},\bm{\mu}\rangle}{24\sigma_{p}\cdot\|(\mathbf{I}-\bm{\mu}\bm{\mu}^{\top}/\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2})\mathbf{w}_{r^{\prime}}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{2}\cdot\sqrt{\log(dn)}}\geq\frac{M^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\log(1/\sigma_{0})^{\frac{1}{q-2}}}{n^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\mathrm{SNR}^{\frac{2}{q-2}}}

for all
r\in\mathcal{I}^{+}
and all
r^{\prime}\in[2m]
. By (
B.39
), it is clear that
\gamma_{r}\geq 2\sigma_{0}
for all
r\in\mathcal{I}^{+}
. This further implies that

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma_{r}/\log(2/\gamma_{r})^{\frac{1}{q-2}}}{|\rho_{{r^{\prime}},i}|}\geq\frac{M^{\frac{1}{q-2}}}{n^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\mathrm{SNR}^{\frac{2}{q-2}}}

for all
r\in\mathcal{I}^{+}
and all
r^{\prime}\in[2m]
.
With exactly the same proof, we can also show that

\displaystyle\frac{-\gamma_{r}/\log(-2/\gamma_{r})^{\frac{1}{q-2}}}{|\rho_{{r^{\prime}},i}|}\geq\frac{M^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\log(1/\sigma_{0})^{\frac{1}{q-2}}}{n^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\mathrm{SNR}^{\frac{2}{q-2}}}

for all
r\in\mathcal{I}^{-}
and all
r^{\prime}\in[2m]
.

Finally, for all
r\in[2m]
, by Lemma
5.1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t+1)}=\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)}+(\mathbf{A}+\bm{\Xi}^{(t)})\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(t)}

for
t=0,\ldots,T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}
.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{w}_{r}^{\perp}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq(1+(1+\sigma_{0})\cdot\lambda_{1})^{T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}\cdot\|\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(0)}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq(1+(1+\sigma_{0})\cdot\lambda_{1})^{T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}\cdot 2\sigma_{0}\cdot\sqrt{d}

\displaystyle\leq(1+(1-\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})\cdot\lambda_{1})^{\frac{1+\sigma_{0}}{1-\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}\cdot 2\sigma_{0}\cdot\sqrt{d}

\displaystyle\leq(1+(1-\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})\cdot\lambda_{1})^{2T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}\cdot 2\sigma_{0}\cdot\sqrt{d}

where the fourth inequality follows by the assumption that
\sigma_{0},\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}\leq 1/4
. Now by the definition of
T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}
, we know that

\displaystyle[1+(1-\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})\lambda_{1}]^{T_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}\leq\max\Bigg\{288M^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\cdot\frac{\log(2/\sigma_{0})^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\cdot\sqrt{\log(dn)}\cdot\sqrt{\log(md)}}{n^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\mathrm{SNR}^{\frac{q}{q-2}}},2\Bigg\}.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{w}_{r}^{\perp}\|_{2}\leq 2\sigma_{0}\cdot\sqrt{d}\cdot\max\Bigg\{288M^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\cdot\frac{\log(2/\sigma_{0})^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\cdot\sqrt{\log(dn)}\cdot\sqrt{\log(md)}}{n^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\mathrm{SNR}^{\frac{q}{q-2}}},2\Bigg\}\leq\frac{1}{n},

where we implement the assumption that
\sigma_{0}\leq d^{-1/2}n^{-1}/4
and

\displaystyle\sigma_{0}\leq\frac{d^{-1/2}n^{-1}\cdot n^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\mathrm{SNR}^{\frac{q}{q-2}}}{576M^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\log(2/\sigma_{0})^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\cdot\sqrt{\log(dn)}\cdot\sqrt{\log(md)}}.

This finishes the proof.
∎

B.2
Proofs of lemmas in Appendix
B.1.1

B.2.1
Proof of Lemma
B.1

Proof of Lemma
B.1
.

Since
\bm{\xi}_{i},i\in[n_{0}]
i.i.d follows
N(\mathbf{0},\sigma_{p}^{2}\cdot(\mathbf{I}-\bm{\mu}\bm{\mu}^{\top}\cdot\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{-2}))
, thus

\left\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\right\|^{2}_{2}
is sub-exponential random variable with

\displaystyle\left\|\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\right\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq\overline{C}_{2}\sigma_{p}^{2},

where
\overline{C}_{2}
is an absolute constant. By Bernstein inequality, with the probability of at least
1-\widetilde{\delta}/(2n_{0})
we have that

\displaystyle-\frac{\overline{C}_{2}}{\sqrt{c}}\sigma_{p}^{2}\sqrt{d\log(4n_{0}/\widetilde{\delta})}\leq\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-d\sigma_{p}^{2}\leq\frac{\overline{C}_{2}}{\sqrt{c}}\sigma_{p}^{2}\sqrt{d\log(4n_{0}/\widetilde{\delta})},

where
c
is also an absolute constant, and it is equivalent to

\displaystyle d\sigma_{p}^{2}-C_{2}\sigma_{p}^{2}\sqrt{d\log(4n_{0}/\widetilde{\delta})}\leq\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\leq d\sigma_{p}^{2}+C_{2}\sigma_{p}^{2}\sqrt{d\log(4n_{0}/\widetilde{\delta})},

where
C_{2}
is an absolute constant that does not depend on other variables.
Similarly, we could obtain that with the probability of at least
1-\widetilde{\delta}/(2n_{0})
,

\displaystyle d\sigma_{p}^{2}-C_{2}\sigma_{p}^{2}\sqrt{d\log(4n_{0}/\widetilde{\delta})}\leq\|\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\leq d\sigma_{p}^{2}+C_{2}\sigma_{p}^{2}\sqrt{d\log(4n_{0}/\widetilde{\delta})},

Apply a union bound for
\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{2},~\|\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}\|_{2}^{2},~i\in[n_{0}]
finishes the proof of this lemma.
∎

B.3
Proofs of lemmas in Appendix
B.1.2

B.3.1
Proof of Lemma
B.2

In this section, the following Lemma
B.5
,
B.6
and
B.7
are introduced to prove Lemma
B.2
.

Lemma B.5
.

Suppose that
\widetilde{\delta}>0
, then with probability at least
1-\widetilde{\delta}
,

\displaystyle|\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}\frac{1}{n_{0}}y_{i}|\leq\sqrt{\frac{2}{n_{0}}\log(2/\widetilde{\delta})}

Proof of Lemma
B.5
.

Since
y_{i},i\in[n_{0}]
independent and identically follow Bernoulli distribution, then by Hoeffding inequality, with the probability of at least
1-\widetilde{\delta}
, we have

\displaystyle|\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}\frac{1}{n_{0}}y_{i}|\leq\sqrt{\frac{2}{n_{0}}\log(2/\widetilde{\delta})}

∎

Lemma B.6
.

For any
\widetilde{\delta}>0
, with probability at least
1-\widetilde{\delta}
, it holds that

\displaystyle dn_{0}\sigma_{p}^{2}-C_{1}n_{0}\sigma_{p}^{2}\sqrt{d\log(2/\widetilde{\delta})}\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}\bm{\xi}_{i}\right\|^{2}_{2}\leq dn_{0}\sigma_{p}^{2}+C_{1}n_{0}\sigma_{p}^{2}\sqrt{d\log(2/\widetilde{\delta})}

\displaystyle dn_{0}\sigma_{p}^{2}-C_{1}n_{0}\sigma_{p}^{2}\sqrt{d\log(2/\widetilde{\delta})}\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}y_{i}\bm{\xi}_{i}\right\|^{2}_{2}\leq dn_{0}\sigma_{p}^{2}+C_{1}n_{0}\sigma_{p}^{2}\sqrt{d\log(2/\widetilde{\delta})}

\displaystyle dn_{0}\sigma_{p}^{2}-C_{1}n_{0}\sigma_{p}^{2}\sqrt{d\log(2/\widetilde{\delta})}\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}y_{i}\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}\right\|^{2}_{2}\leq dn_{0}\sigma_{p}^{2}+C_{1}n_{0}\sigma_{p}^{2}\sqrt{d\log(2/\widetilde{\delta})}

where
C_{1}
is an absolute constant that does not depend on other variables.

Proof of Lemma
B.6
.

Since
\bm{\xi}_{i},i\in[n_{0}]
i.i.d follows
N(\mathbf{0},\sigma_{p}^{2}\cdot(\mathbf{I}-\bm{\mu}\bm{\mu}^{\top}\cdot\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{-2}))
, therefore
\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}\bm{\xi}_{i}\sim N(\mathbf{0},n_{0}\sigma_{p}^{2}\cdot(\mathbf{I}-\bm{\mu}\bm{\mu}^{\top}\cdot\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{-2}))
, thus

\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}\bm{\xi}_{i}\right\|^{2}_{2}
is sub-exponential random variable with

\displaystyle\left\|\bigg\|\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}\bm{\xi}_{i}\bigg\|_{2}^{2}\right\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq\overline{C}_{1}\sigma_{p}^{2}n_{0},

where
\overline{C}_{1}
is an absolute constant.
By Bernstein inequality, with the probability of at least
1-\widetilde{\delta}
we have

\displaystyle-\frac{\overline{C}_{1}}{\sqrt{c}}n_{0}\sigma_{p}^{2}\sqrt{d\log(2/\widetilde{\delta})}\leq\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}\bm{\xi}_{i}\right\|^{2}_{2}-dn_{0}\sigma_{p}^{2}\leq\frac{\overline{C}_{1}}{\sqrt{c}}n_{0}\sigma_{p}^{2}\sqrt{d\log(2/\widetilde{\delta})},

where
c
is also an absolute constant, and it is equivalent to

\displaystyle dn_{0}\sigma_{p}^{2}-C_{1}n_{0}\sigma_{p}^{2}\sqrt{d\log(2/\widetilde{\delta})}\leq\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}\bm{\xi}_{i}\right\|^{2}_{2}\leq dn_{0}\sigma_{p}^{2}+C_{1}n_{0}\sigma_{p}^{2}\sqrt{d\log(2/\widetilde{\delta})},

where
C_{1}
is an absolute constant, which does not depend on other variables.
Notice that similar results could be proved for
\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}y_{i}\bm{\xi}_{i}\right\|^{2}_{2}
and
\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}y_{i}\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}\right\|^{2}_{2}
.
∎

Lemma B.7
.

For any
\widetilde{\delta}>0
, with probability at least
1-\widetilde{\delta}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\Bigg\|\frac{1}{n_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}(\bm{\xi}_{i}\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}^{\top}+\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}\bm{\xi}_{i}^{\top})\Bigg\|_{2}\leq C_{3}\sigma_{p}^{2}\cdot\max\Bigg\{\sqrt{\frac{d\log(9/\widetilde{\delta})}{n_{0}}},\frac{d\log(9/\widetilde{\delta})}{n_{0}}\Bigg\},

where
C_{3}
is an absolute constant.

Proof of Lemma
B.7
.

Within this proof, we denote function

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}=\frac{1}{n_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}(\bm{\xi}_{i}\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}^{\top}+\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}\bm{\xi}_{i}^{\top}),

and

\displaystyle g(\mathbf{a})=\mathbf{a}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{a},

for all
\mathbf{a}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. By Lemma 5.2 in
Vershynin (2010)
, there exists a
1/4
-net
\mathcal{N}
covering the
d
-dimensional unit sphere
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
with
|\mathcal{N}|\leq 9^{d}
. Then for any
\mathbf{a}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, there exists
\widehat{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{N}\subseteq\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
such that
\|\widehat{\mathbf{a}}-\mathbf{a}\|_{2}\leq 1/4
.

Now for any fixed
\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{0}\in\mathcal{N}
, with direct calculation we have

\displaystyle g(\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{0})=\frac{2}{n_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}\langle\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{0},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\cdot\langle\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{0},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}\rangle.

Since
\|\widehat{\mathbf{a}}\|_{2}=1
,
\langle\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{0},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle,\langle\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{0},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
are independent
N(0,\sigma_{p})
random variables,
i=1,\ldots,n_{0}
. Therefore, by Lemma 5.14 in
Vershynin (2010)
,
\langle\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{0},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\cdot\langle\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{0},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
is sub-exponential with

\displaystyle\|\langle\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{0},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\cdot\langle\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{0},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq c_{1}\cdot\sigma_{p}^{2},

where
c_{1}
is an absolute constant. Then by Bernstein-type inequality (Proposition 5.16 in
Vershynin (2010)
), with probability at least
1-9^{-d}\widetilde{\delta}
, we have

\displaystyle|g(\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{0})|

\displaystyle=\Bigg|\frac{2}{n_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}\langle\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{0},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\cdot\langle\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{0},\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\Bigg|\leq 2c_{1}\sigma_{p}^{2}\cdot\max\Bigg\{\sqrt{\frac{\log(9^{d}/\widetilde{\delta})}{n_{0}}},\frac{\log(9^{d}/\widetilde{\delta})}{n_{0}}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq 2c_{1}\sigma_{p}^{2}\cdot\max\Bigg\{\sqrt{\frac{d\log(9/\widetilde{\delta})}{n_{0}}},\frac{d\log(9/\widetilde{\delta})}{n_{0}}\Bigg\}.

Since the above conclusion holds for arbitrary
\widehat{\mathbf{a}}_{0}\in\mathcal{N}
, by union bound, with probability at least
1-\widetilde{\delta}
we have

\displaystyle|g(\widehat{\mathbf{a}})|\leq 2c_{1}\sigma_{p}^{2}\cdot\max\Bigg\{\sqrt{\frac{\log(9^{d}/\widetilde{\delta})}{n_{0}}},\frac{\log(9^{d}/\widetilde{\delta})}{n_{0}}\Bigg\}\leq 2c_{1}\sigma_{p}^{2}\cdot\max\Bigg\{\sqrt{\frac{d\log(9/\widetilde{\delta})}{n_{0}}},\frac{d\log(9/\widetilde{\delta})}{n_{0}}\Bigg\}

for all
\widehat{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{N}
. Now for any
\mathbf{a}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, there exists
\widehat{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{N}
such that
\|\widehat{\mathbf{a}}-\mathbf{a}\|_{2}\leq 1/4
, and hence

\displaystyle|g(\mathbf{a})|

\displaystyle\leq|g(\widehat{\mathbf{a}})|+|g(\mathbf{a})-g(\widehat{\mathbf{a}})|

\displaystyle=|g(\widehat{\mathbf{a}})|+|\mathbf{a}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{a}-\widehat{\mathbf{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\widehat{\mathbf{a}}|

\displaystyle\leq 2c_{1}\sigma_{p}^{2}\cdot\max\Bigg\{\sqrt{\frac{d\log(9/\widetilde{\delta})}{n_{0}}},\frac{d\log(9/\widetilde{\delta})}{n_{0}}\Bigg\}+|\mathbf{a}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\widehat{\mathbf{a}}|+|\mathbf{a}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\widehat{\mathbf{a}}-\widehat{\mathbf{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\widehat{\mathbf{a}}|

\displaystyle\leq 2c_{1}\sigma_{p}^{2}\cdot\max\Bigg\{\sqrt{\frac{d\log(9/\widetilde{\delta})}{n_{0}}},\frac{d\log(9/\widetilde{\delta})}{n_{0}}\Bigg\}+|\mathbf{a}^{\top}\mathbf{M}(\mathbf{a}-\widehat{\mathbf{a}})|+|(\mathbf{a}-\widehat{\mathbf{a}})^{\top}\mathbf{M}\widehat{\mathbf{a}}|

By Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle|\mathbf{a}^{\top}\mathbf{M}(\mathbf{a}-\widehat{\mathbf{a}})|\leq\sqrt{\mathbf{a}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{a}}\cdot\sqrt{(\mathbf{a}-\widehat{\mathbf{a}})^{\top}\mathbf{M}(\mathbf{a}-\widehat{\mathbf{a}})}=\sqrt{g(\mathbf{a})}\cdot\|\mathbf{a}-\widehat{\mathbf{a}}\|_{2}\cdot\sqrt{g(\mathbf{a}-\widehat{\mathbf{a}})},

\displaystyle|(\mathbf{a}-\widehat{\mathbf{a}})^{\top}\mathbf{M}\widehat{\mathbf{a}}|\leq\sqrt{\widehat{\mathbf{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\widehat{\mathbf{a}}}\cdot\sqrt{(\mathbf{a}-\widehat{\mathbf{a}})^{\top}\mathbf{M}(\mathbf{a}-\widehat{\mathbf{a}})}=\sqrt{g(\widehat{\mathbf{a}})}\cdot\|\mathbf{a}-\widehat{\mathbf{a}}\|_{2}\cdot\sqrt{g(\mathbf{a}-\widehat{\mathbf{a}})}.

Therefore, we further have

\displaystyle|g(\mathbf{a})|

\displaystyle\leq 2c_{1}\sigma_{p}^{2}\cdot\max\Bigg\{\sqrt{\frac{d\log(9/\widetilde{\delta})}{n_{0}}},\frac{d\log(9/\widetilde{\delta})}{n_{0}}\Bigg\}+\sqrt{g(\mathbf{a})}\cdot\|\mathbf{a}-\widehat{\mathbf{a}}\|_{2}\cdot\sqrt{g(\mathbf{a}-\widehat{\mathbf{a}})}

\displaystyle\quad+\sqrt{g(\widehat{\mathbf{a}})}\cdot\|\mathbf{a}-\widehat{\mathbf{a}}\|_{2}\cdot\sqrt{g(\mathbf{a}-\widehat{\mathbf{a}})}

\displaystyle\leq 2c_{1}\sigma_{p}^{2}\cdot\max\Bigg\{\sqrt{\frac{d\log(9/\widetilde{\delta})}{n_{0}}},\frac{d\log(9/\widetilde{\delta})}{n_{0}}\Bigg\}+\frac{1}{4}\cdot\sup_{\mathbf{a}}g(\mathbf{a})+\frac{1}{4}\cdot\sup_{\mathbf{a}}g(\mathbf{a})

\displaystyle=2c_{1}\sigma_{p}^{2}\cdot\max\Bigg\{\sqrt{\frac{d\log(9/\widetilde{\delta})}{n_{0}}},\frac{d\log(9/\widetilde{\delta})}{n_{0}}\Bigg\}+\frac{1}{2}\cdot\sup_{\mathbf{a}}g(\mathbf{a})

for all
\mathbf{a}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
.
Taking a supremum then gives

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{a}}|g(\mathbf{a})|\leq 2c_{1}\sigma_{p}^{2}\cdot\max\Bigg\{\sqrt{\frac{d\log(9/\widetilde{\delta})}{n_{0}}},\frac{d\log(9/\widetilde{\delta})}{n_{0}}\Bigg\}+\frac{1}{2}\cdot\sup_{\mathbf{a}}g(\mathbf{a}).

Therefore, we conclude that

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{a}}|g(\mathbf{a})|\leq 4c_{1}\sigma_{p}^{2}\cdot\max\Bigg\{\sqrt{\frac{d\log(9/\widetilde{\delta})}{n_{0}}},\frac{d\log(9/\widetilde{\delta})}{n_{0}}\Bigg\}.

This finishes the proof.
∎

Based on Lemmas
B.5
,
B.6
and
B.7
, the following Lemma
B.2
is proved.

Proof of Lemma
B.2
.

The matrix
\mathbf{A}
defined in Lemma
5.1
can be written and simplified in the following way.

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}=

\displaystyle-\frac{\eta}{n_{0}^{2}\tau}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}\sum_{i^{\prime}\neq i}(\mathbf{z}_{i}\mathbf{z}_{i^{\prime}}^{\top}+\mathbf{z}_{i^{\prime}}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\top}-\mathbf{z}_{i}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}_{i}}^{\top}-\widetilde{\mathbf{z}_{i}}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\top})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\eta}{n_{0}^{2}\tau}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}\sum_{i^{\prime}\neq i}\big[2(y_{i}y_{i^{\prime}}-1)\bm{\mu}\bm{\mu}^{\top}+y_{i}(\bm{\mu}\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}^{\top}+\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}\bm{\mu}^{\top})+y_{i^{\prime}}(\bm{\mu}\bm{\xi}_{i}^{\top}+\bm{\xi}_{i}\bm{\mu}^{\top})

\displaystyle-y_{i}(\bm{\mu}\widetilde{\bm{\xi}_{i}}^{\top}+\widetilde{\bm{\xi}_{i}}\bm{\mu}^{\top}+\bm{\mu}\bm{\xi}_{i}^{\top}+\bm{\xi}_{i}\bm{\mu}^{\top})+\bm{\xi}_{i}\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}^{\top}+\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}\bm{\xi}_{i}^{\top}-\bm{\xi}_{i}\widetilde{\bm{\xi}_{i}}^{\top}-\widetilde{\bm{\xi}_{i}}\bm{\xi}_{i}^{\top}\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\eta}{n_{0}^{2}\tau}\left\{\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}\sum_{i^{\prime}\neq i}2(y_{i}y_{i^{\prime}}-1)\right]\bm{\mu}\bm{\mu}^{\top}+2(\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}y_{i})\bm{\mu}(\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n_{0}}\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}})^{\top}+2(\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}y_{i})(\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n_{0}}\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}})\bm{\mu}^{\top}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad-(n_{0}-1)\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}(y_{i}\bm{\mu}\widetilde{\bm{\xi}_{i}}^{\top}+y_{i}\widetilde{\bm{\xi}_{i}}\bm{\mu}^{\top})-(n_{0}+1)\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}(y_{i}\bm{\mu}\bm{\xi}_{i}^{\top}+y_{i}\bm{\xi}_{i}\bm{\mu}^{\top})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\left.+\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}\sum_{i^{\prime}\neq i}(\bm{\xi}_{i}\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}^{\top}+\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}\bm{\xi}_{i}^{\top})-\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}(n_{0}-1)\bm{\xi}_{i}\widetilde{\bm{\xi}_{i}}^{\top}-\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}(n_{0}-1)\widetilde{\bm{\xi}_{i}}\bm{\xi}_{i}^{\top}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\eta}{n_{0}^{2}\tau}\left\{\left[2(\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}y_{i})^{2}-2n_{0}^{2}\right]\bm{\mu}\bm{\mu}^{\top}+2(\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}y_{i})\bm{\mu}(\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n_{0}}\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}})^{\top}+2(\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}y_{i})(\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n_{0}}\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}})\bm{\mu}^{\top}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad-(n_{0}-1)\big[\bm{\mu}(\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}y_{i}\widetilde{\bm{\xi}_{i}})^{\top}+(\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}y_{i}\widetilde{\bm{\xi}_{i}})\bm{\mu}^{\top}\big]-(n_{0}+1)\big[\bm{\mu}(\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}y_{i}\bm{\xi}_{i})^{\top}+(\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}y_{i}\bm{\xi}_{i})\bm{\mu}^{\top}\big]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\left.+2(\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}\bm{\xi}_{i})(\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}\bm{\xi}_{i})^{\top}-2\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}\bm{\xi}_{i}\bm{\xi}_{i}^{\top}-\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}(n_{0}-1)\bm{\xi}_{i}\widetilde{\bm{\xi}_{i}}^{\top}-\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}(n_{0}-1)\widetilde{\bm{\xi}_{i}}\bm{\xi}_{i}^{\top}\right\}.

Then by definition, we have

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}=-\frac{\eta}{n_{0}^{2}\tau}[\bm{\Delta}_{1}-\bm{\Delta}_{2}-\bm{\Delta}_{3}+\bm{\Delta}_{4}-\bm{\Delta}_{5}-\bm{\Delta}_{6}],

where

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}_{1}=2(\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}y_{i})\bm{\mu}(\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n_{0}}\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}})^{\top}+2(\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}y_{i})(\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n_{0}}\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}})\bm{\mu}^{\top},

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}_{2}=(n_{0}-1)[\bm{\mu}(\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}y_{i}\widetilde{\bm{\xi}_{i}})^{\top}+(\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}y_{i}\widetilde{\bm{\xi}_{i}})\bm{\mu}^{\top}],

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}_{3}=(n_{0}+1)[\bm{\mu}(\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}y_{i}\bm{\xi}_{i})^{\top}+(\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}y_{i}\bm{\xi}_{i})\bm{\mu}^{\top}],

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}_{4}=2(\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}\bm{\xi}_{i})(\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}\bm{\xi}_{i})^{\top},

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}_{5}=2\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}\bm{\xi}_{i}\bm{\xi}_{i}^{\top},

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}_{6}=(n_{0}-1)\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}\bm{\xi}_{i}\widetilde{\bm{\xi}_{i}}^{\top}+(n_{0}-1)\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}\widetilde{\bm{\xi}_{i}}\bm{\xi}_{i}^{\top}.

Thus,
\|\bm{\Delta}_{1}\|_{2}
can be bounded as follows,

\displaystyle\|\bm{\Delta}_{1}\|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle 4\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}y_{i}\right|\cdot\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}\cdot\Bigg\|\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n_{0}}\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}\Bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4\sqrt{2n_{0}}\cdot\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}\cdot\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{p}\sqrt{n_{0}d}),

(B.51)

where the second inequality is by
\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}y_{i}\right|\leq\sqrt{2n_{0}\log(2/\widetilde{\delta})}
in Lemma
B.5
, and

\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}\bm{\xi}_{i}\right\|^{2}_{2}\leq dn_{0}\sigma_{p}^{2}+c_{1}n_{0}\sigma_{p}^{2}\sqrt{d\log(2/\widetilde{\delta})}
in Lemma
B.6
.
Also,
\bm{\Delta}_{2},\bm{\Delta}_{3},\bm{\Delta}_{4}
are handled similarly as
\bm{\Delta}_{1}
, namely

\displaystyle\|\bm{\Delta}_{2}\|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle 2(n_{0}-1)\cdot\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}\cdot\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}y_{i}\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2(n_{0}-1)\cdot\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}\cdot\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{p}\sqrt{n_{0}d})

(B.52)

where the second inequality is by

\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}y_{i}\widetilde{\bm{\xi}}_{i}\right\|^{2}_{2}\leq dn_{0}\sigma_{p}^{2}+c_{1}n_{0}\sigma_{p}^{2}\sqrt{d\log(2/\widetilde{\delta})}
in Lemma
B.6
.

\displaystyle\|\bm{\Delta}_{3}\|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle 2(n_{0}+1)\cdot\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}\cdot\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}y_{i}\bm{\xi}_{i}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2(n_{0}+1)\cdot\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}\cdot\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{p}\sqrt{n_{0}d})

(B.53)

where the second inequality is by

\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}y_{i}\bm{\xi}_{i}\right\|^{2}_{2}\leq dn_{0}\sigma_{p}^{2}+c_{1}n_{0}\sigma_{p}^{2}\sqrt{d\log(2/\widetilde{\delta})}
in Lemma
B.6
.

\displaystyle\|\bm{\Delta}_{4}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|2(\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}\bm{\xi}_{i})(\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}\bm{\xi}_{i})^{\top}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\cdot\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}\bm{\xi}_{i}\right\|^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{p}^{2}n_{0}d)

(B.54)

where the second inequality is by

\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}\bm{\xi}_{i}\right\|^{2}_{2}\leq dn_{0}\sigma_{p}^{2}+c_{1}n_{0}\sigma_{p}^{2}\sqrt{d\log(2/\widetilde{\delta})}
in Lemma
B.6
.

For
\bm{\Delta}_{5}
, by Theorem 5.39 in
Vershynin (2010)
, with probability at least
1-\widetilde{\delta}
, we have that

\displaystyle\|\bm{\Delta}_{5}\|_{2}=

\displaystyle 2\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n_{0}}\bm{\xi}_{i}\bm{\xi}_{i}^{\top}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\sigma_{p}^{2}\left(\sqrt{d}+c_{4}\sqrt{n_{0}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{c_{4}}}\cdot\sqrt{\log(2/\widetilde{\delta})}\right)^{2}\leq 6\sigma_{p}^{2}\left(d+c_{4}^{2}n_{0}+\frac{1}{c_{4}}\cdot\log(2/\widetilde{\delta})\right),

(B.55)

where
c_{4}
is an absolute constant.
The bound for
\|\bm{\Delta}_{6}\|_{2}
is already proved in Lemma
B.7
.
Therefore, by (
B.51
), (
B.52
), (
B.53
), (
B.54
), (
B.55
), and Lemma
B.7
, set
\widetilde{\delta}=\delta/6
,
with probability at least
1-\delta
, we have

\displaystyle\|\bm{\Delta}\|_{2}=\|\bm{\Sigma}-\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}\|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\eta}{n_{0}^{2}\tau}\left[\|\bm{\Delta}_{1}\|_{2}+\|\bm{\Delta}_{2}\|_{2}+\|\bm{\Delta}_{3}\|_{2}+\|\bm{\Delta}_{4}\|_{2}+\|\bm{\Delta}_{5}\|_{2}+\|\bm{\Delta}_{6}\|_{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\eta}{n_{0}^{2}\tau}\Bigg[4\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}\sqrt{2n_{0}}\cdot\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{p}\sqrt{n_{0}d})+2(n_{0}-1)\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}\cdot\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{p}\sqrt{n_{0}d})

\displaystyle+2(n_{0}+1)\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}\cdot\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{p}\sqrt{n_{0}d})+2\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{p}^{2}n_{0}d)+6\sigma_{p}^{2}\left(d+c_{4}^{2}n_{0}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{c_{4}}^{2}}\cdot\log(2/\widetilde{\delta})\right)

\displaystyle+c_{3}n_{0}^{2}\sigma_{p}^{2}\cdot\max\Bigg\{\sqrt{\frac{d\log(9/\widetilde{\delta})}{n_{0}}},\frac{d\log(9/\widetilde{\delta})}{n_{0}}\Bigg\}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Bigg[4\sqrt{2}\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{-1}\cdot\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d}n_{0}^{-1})+2\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{-1}\cdot\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{0}}})

\displaystyle+2\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{-1}\cdot\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{0}}})+2\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{-2}\cdot\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{p}^{2}dn_{0}^{-1})+\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{-2}\cdot\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{p}^{2}dn_{0}^{-1})

\displaystyle+c_{3}\sigma_{p}^{2}d\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{-2}\cdot\max\Bigg\{\sqrt{\frac{\log(9/\widetilde{\delta})}{dn_{0}}},\frac{\log(9/\widetilde{\delta})}{n_{0}}\Bigg\}\Bigg]\frac{\eta}{\tau}\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2},

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Bigg[\widetilde{O}(\mathrm{SNR}^{-1}\cdot n_{0}^{-1})+\widetilde{O}(\mathrm{SNR}^{-1}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{0}}})+\widetilde{O}(\mathrm{SNR}^{-2}\cdot n_{0}^{-1})

\displaystyle+c_{3}\cdot\mathrm{SNR}^{-2}\cdot\max\Bigg\{\sqrt{\frac{\log(9/\widetilde{\delta})}{dn_{0}}},\frac{\log(9/\widetilde{\delta})}{n_{0}}\Bigg\}\Bigg]\frac{\eta}{\tau}\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2},

where
\mathrm{SNR}=\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}/(\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d})
, and
\frac{\eta}{\tau}\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}
is the lower bound of
\widehat{\lambda}_{1}
proved in Lemma
5.2
.
∎

B.4
Proofs of lemmas in Appendix
B.1.3

B.4.1
Proof of Lemma
B.3

With a proof similar to Lemma B.3 in
Cao et al. (2022)
, we have the following Lemma
B.3
. Although the proof is almost the same as in
Cao et al. (2022)
, since the results are presented in different forms, for self-consistency, we still present the proof of this Lemma
B.3
.

Proof of Lemma
B.3
.

Since
\mathbf{v}
is a unit vector, for each
r\in[2m]
,
j\cdot\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(0)},\mathbf{v}_{j}\rangle
is a Gaussian random variable with mean zero and variance
\sigma_{0}^{2}
. Therefore, by Gaussian tail bound and union bound, with probability at least
1-\delta/2
,

\displaystyle|\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(0)},\mathbf{v}_{j}\rangle|\leq\sqrt{2\log(16mn_{0}/\delta)}\cdot\sigma_{0}

(B.56)

for all
r\in[2m]
and
j\in[d]
. This proves the first part of the result. For the second part of the result,
we note that
\mathbb{P}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(0)},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle\geq\sigma_{0}/2)=\mathbb{P}_{Z\sim N(0,1)}(Z\geq 1/2)\geq 0.3
is an absolute constant. Therefore, binary random variables
\ind\{\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(0)},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle\geq\sigma_{0}/2\}
,
r\in[2m]
are independent
\mathrm{Bernoulli}(p)
random variables with constant
0.3\leq p\leq 0.5
. By Hoeffding’s inequality, with probability at least
1-\delta/4
,

\displaystyle\Bigg|\sum_{r=1}^{2m}\ind\{\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(0)},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle\geq\sigma_{0}/2\}-2mp\Bigg|\leq\sqrt{2m\log(8/\delta)}.

Therefore, with probability at least
1-\delta/4
,

\displaystyle\sum_{r=1}^{2m}\ind\{\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(0)},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle\geq\sigma_{0}/2\}\geq 2mp-\sqrt{2m\log(8/\delta)}\geq 2m/5,

where the last inequality holds by the assumption that
m=\widetilde{\Omega}(1)
. The inequality above implies that there exist distinct
r_{1}^{+},\ldots,r_{2m/5}^{+}\in[2m]
such that
\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(0)},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle\geq\sigma_{0}/2
for all
r\in\{r_{1}^{+},\ldots,r_{2m/5}^{+}\}
.

With exactly the same proof, we also have that, with probability at least
1-\delta/4
, there exist distinct
r_{1}^{-},\ldots,r_{2m/5}^{-}\in[2m]
such that
\langle\mathbf{w}_{r}^{(0)},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle\leq-\sigma_{0}/2
for all
r\in\{r_{1}^{-},\ldots,r_{2m/5}^{-}\}
. It is also clear that as long as the sets
\{r_{1}^{+},\ldots,r_{2m/5}^{+}\}
and
\{r_{1}^{-},\ldots,r_{2m/5}^{-}\}
exist, they must be disjoint. Therefore, applying a union bound finishes the proof.
∎

B.4.2
Proof of Lemma
B.4

Proof of Lemma
B.4
.

The first conclusion that
|\lambda_{i}-\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}|\leq\sigma_{0}\cdot\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2}
,
i\in[n]
directly follows by Weyl’s theorem and the assumption that
\bm{\Xi}
is symmetric and
\|\bm{\Xi}\|_{2}\leq\sigma_{0}\cdot\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2}
.

For the second result, we have

\displaystyle\sin\theta(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|\mathbf{A}-(\mathbf{A}+\bm{\Xi})\|_{2}}{|\widetilde{\lambda}_{2}-\lambda_{1}|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sigma_{0}\cdot\lambda_{1}}{(1-\sigma_{0})\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sigma_{0}\cdot\lambda_{1}}{(1-\sigma_{0})\lambda_{1}-\frac{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}}{2(1-\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}})}\cdot\lambda_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sigma_{0},

where the first inequality follows by the variant of Davis-Kahan Theorem (Theorem 1 in
Yu et al. (2015)
), the second inequality follows by the first conclusion of is lemma (which has been proved above) and the assumption that
\|\bm{\Xi}\|_{2}\leq\sigma_{0}\cdot\lambda_{1}
, the third inequality follows by Lemma
5.2
, and the fourth inequality follows by the assumption that
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{SimCLR}}\leq 1/4
and
\sigma_{0}\leq 1/4
. Then we have

\displaystyle|\langle\mathbf{v}_{1},\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{1}\rangle|=\sqrt{1-\sin^{2}\theta(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1})}\geq\sqrt{1-4\sigma_{0}^{2}}\geq 1-4\sigma_{0}^{2}.

This proves the second conclusion. For the last result, for
i=2,\ldots,d
, since
\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{i}
is perpendicular to
\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle|\langle\mathbf{v}_{1},\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{i}\rangle|

\displaystyle=|\langle\mathbf{v}_{1},(\mathbf{I}-\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{1}\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{1}^{\top}+\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{1}\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{1}^{\top})\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{i}\rangle|

\displaystyle=|\langle\mathbf{v}_{1},(\mathbf{I}-\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{1}\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{1}^{\top})\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{i}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq\|(\mathbf{I}-\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{1}\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{1}^{\top})\mathbf{v}_{1}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{1-\langle\mathbf{v}_{1},\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{1}\rangle^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{1-(1-4\sigma_{0}^{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{8\sigma_{0}^{2}-16\sigma_{0}^{4}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{8\sigma_{0}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 4\sigma_{0}.

To prove the last inequality, we have

\displaystyle\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{v}_{1}\mathbf{v}_{1}^{\top})\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{1}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{1}-\langle\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle\cdot\mathbf{v}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{1-2\langle\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle^{2}+\langle\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle^{2}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{1-\langle\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1}\rangle^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{1-(1-4\sigma_{0}^{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{8\sigma_{0}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 4\sigma_{0}.

This finishes the proof.
∎

Appendix C
Proofs for supervised fine-tuning

In this section, the training process of the fine-tuning stage is investigated.
We first present the basic setting and the decomposition of coefficients.

The initialization
\mathbf{W}^{(0)}
of the fine-tuning stage is derived by the pre-training stage, and will be fine-tuned in the following stage based on the CNN model (
3.2
). It can be directly decomposed as

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbf{w}}_{jr}^{(0)}=\mathbf{w}_{jr}^{\perp}+j\cdot\gamma_{jr}^{(0)}\cdot\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{-2}\cdot\bm{\mu}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\rho_{jri}^{(0)}\cdot\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{-2}\cdot\bm{\xi}_{i},

(C.1)

where
\mathbf{w}_{jr}^{\perp}
is a component of
\overline{\mathbf{w}}_{jr}^{(0)}
perpendicular with
\langle\mathbf{w}_{jr}^{\perp},\bm{\mu}\rangle=0,~\langle\mathbf{w}_{jr}^{\perp},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle=0,i\in[n]
, and we have
\max_{j,r}\|\mathbf{w}_{jr}^{\perp}\|_{2}\leq 1/n
by Theorem
5.3
.

In the fine-tuning stage, based on the gradient descent algorithm and the CNN structure defined in (
3.2
), the updating rules of
\mathbf{w}_{j,r},j\in\{-1,+1\},r\in[m]
is given as

\displaystyle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t+1)}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)}-\eta\cdot\nabla_{\mathbf{w}_{j,r}}L_{S}(\bm{W}^{(t)})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)}-\frac{\eta}{nm}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell_{i}^{\prime(t)}\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle)\cdot jy_{i}\bm{\xi}_{i}-\frac{\eta}{nm}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell_{i}^{\prime(t)}\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y_{i}\bm{\mu}\rangle)\cdot j\bm{\mu},

where
\ell_{i}^{\prime(t)}=\ell^{\prime}[y_{i}\cdot f(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i})]
.

The convolution filters
\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},~r\in[m],~j\in\{+1,-1\}
can be decomposed into the following format. There exist unique coefficient
\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)}
and
\rho_{j,r,i}^{(t)}
such that

\displaystyle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)}=\mathbf{w}_{jr}^{\perp}+j\cdot\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)}\cdot\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{-2}\cdot\bm{\mu}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\rho_{j,r,i}^{(t)}\cdot\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{-2}\cdot\bm{\xi}_{i},~~t\geq 0.

(C.2)

If further decompose
\rho_{j,r,i}^{(t)}
into
\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}:=\rho_{j,r,i}^{(t)}\ind(\rho_{j,r,i}^{(t)}\geq 0)
,
\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}:=\rho_{j,r,i}^{(t)}\ind(\rho_{j,r,i}^{(t)}\leq 0)
,
then the decomposition of
\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)}
can be converted into

\displaystyle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)}=\mathbf{w}_{jr}^{\perp}+j\cdot\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)}\cdot\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{-2}\cdot\bm{\mu}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}\cdot\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{-2}\cdot\bm{\xi}_{i}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}\cdot\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{-2}\cdot\bm{\xi}_{i},~~t\geq 0.

(C.3)

Based on the updating rules (
3.3
) and decomposition (
C.3
) of
\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)}
,
the updating rules of coefficients
\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)},\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)},\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}
are as follows

\displaystyle\gamma_{j,r}^{(0)},\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(0)},\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(0)}\neq 0,

(C.4)

\displaystyle\gamma_{j,r}^{(t+1)}=\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)}-\frac{\eta}{nm}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell_{i}^{\prime(t)}\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y_{i}\cdot\bm{\mu}\rangle)\cdot\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2},

(C.5)

\displaystyle\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t+1)}=\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}-\frac{\eta}{nm}\cdot\ell_{i}^{\prime(t)}\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle)\cdot\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\ind(y_{i}=j),

(C.6)

\displaystyle\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t+1)}=\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}+\frac{\eta}{nm}\cdot\ell_{i}^{\prime(t)}\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle)\cdot\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\ind(y_{i}=-j).

(C.7)

It follows that by substitute
\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y_{i}\cdot\bm{\mu}\rangle,~\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle
in (
C.5
)-(
C.7
), we have,

\displaystyle\gamma_{j,r}^{(0)},\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(0)},\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(0)}\neq 0,

\displaystyle\gamma_{j,r}^{(t+1)}=\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)}-\frac{\eta}{nm}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell_{i}^{\prime(t)}\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(jy_{i}\cdot\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)})\cdot\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2},

\displaystyle\rho_{j,r,i}^{(t+1)}=\rho_{j,r,i}^{(t)}-\frac{\eta}{nm}\cdot\ell_{i}^{\prime(t)}\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}\rho_{j,r,i^{\prime}}^{(t)}\frac{\langle\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle}{\|\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}\|_{2}^{2}})\cdot\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot,

\displaystyle\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t+1)}=\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}-\frac{\eta}{nm}\cdot\ell_{i}^{\prime(t)}\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i^{\prime}}^{(t)}\frac{\langle\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle}{\|\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}\|_{2}^{2}}+\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i^{\prime}}^{(t)}\frac{\langle\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle}{\|\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}\|_{2}^{2}})\cdot\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\ind(y_{i}=j),

\displaystyle\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t+1)}=\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}+\frac{\eta}{nm}\cdot\ell_{i}^{\prime(t)}\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i^{\prime}}^{(t)}\frac{\langle\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle}{\|\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}\|_{2}^{2}}+\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i^{\prime}}^{(t)}\frac{\langle\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle}{\|\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}\|_{2}^{2}})\cdot\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\ind(y_{i}=-j).

(C.8)

The coefficients initialization (
C.4
) is determined by the pre-training stage, which is given in (
C.1
),
while the one-step updating rules for the coefficients are not influenced by the initialization.

Denote
T^{*}=\eta^{-1}\text{poly}(\epsilon^{-1},\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{-1},d^{-1}\sigma_{p}^{-2},n,m,d)
the maximum admissible iterations.

Based on the result of pre-training stage in Theorem
5.3
, it is easy to verify that the following assumptions hold.

Assumption C.1

(Assumptions on the scale of initialization)
.

Assume the following equations hold,

\displaystyle-4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{\ast})\leq-C_{0}\leq\gamma_{j,r}^{(0)}\leq 4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{\ast})

\displaystyle 0\leq\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(0)}\leq 4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{\ast})

\displaystyle 0\geq\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(0)}\geq-64nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot\log(T^{\ast})

for all
r\in[m]
,
j\in\{\pm 1\}
and all
i\in[n]
, where
C_{0}
is constant such that
0\leq C_{0}\leq 4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{\ast})
.

Assumption C.2

(Assumptions on the initialization of
\gamma
)
.

There exists at least one index
r_{1}\in[m]
such that
\gamma_{1,r_{1}}^{(0)}\geq\gamma_{0}
, and
there exists at least one index
r_{2}\in[m]
such that
\gamma_{-1,r_{2}}^{(0)}\geq\gamma_{0}
. Furthermore, we require that

\displaystyle\max_{j,r}\{0,(-\gamma^{(0)}_{j,r})^{q}\}\ll 4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{\ast})

(C.9)

C.1
Proof of Theorem
5.5

In this section, in order to prove Theorem
5.5
, the following Lemma
C.3
-
C.6
are introduced to analyze the signal learning in the fine-tuning stage.
Two stages of the signal learning as well as the population loss are analyzed here.

C.1.1
First Stage of signal learning

Lemma C.3
.

Under the same conditions as Theorem
5.5
, in particular if the SNR satisfies that

\displaystyle\mathrm{SNR}^{2}\geq\frac{4\log(2/\gamma_{0})8^{q}\rho_{0}^{q-2}}{C_{1}n\gamma_{0}^{q-2}}

(C.10)

where
C_{1}=O(1)
is a positive constant, there exists time

\displaystyle T_{1}=\frac{\log(2/\gamma_{0})8m}{C_{1}\eta q\gamma_{0}^{q-2}\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}}

such that

\displaystyle\max_{r}\gamma_{j,r}^{(T_{1})}\geq 2,~\text{for}~j\in\{\pm 1\}.

(C.11)

\displaystyle|\rho_{j,r,i}^{(t)}|\leq 2\rho_{0},~\text{for all}~j\in\{\pm 1\},r\in[m],i\in[n],0\leq t\leq T_{1}.

(C.12)

where
\rho_{0}=\max_{j,r,i}|\rho_{j,r,i}^{(0)}|
,
\gamma_{0}
is as defined in Assumption
C.3
.

C.1.2
Second Stage of signal learning

Based on the result of First Stage (Section
C.1.1
) in Lemma
C.3
, at the beginning of the second stage, we have the following properties,

•

\max_{r}\gamma_{j,r}^{(T_{1})}\geq 2,~j\in\{\pm 1\}
.

•

\max_{j,r,i}|\rho_{j,r,i}^{(T_{1})}|\leq 2\rho_{0}
.

The learned feature
\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)}
will not get worse, i.e., for
t\geq T_{1}
, we have that
\gamma_{j,r}^{(t+1)}\geq\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)}
, and therefore
\max_{r}\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)}\geq 2
. Now we choose
\mathbf{W}^{*}
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbf{w}^{*}_{j,r}=\mathbf{w}_{jr}^{\perp}+2qm\log(2q/\epsilon)\cdot j\cdot\frac{\bm{\mu}}{\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}},~~j\in\{+1,-1\},~r\in[m].

(C.13)

Based on the above definition of
\mathbf{W}^{*}
, we have the following Lemma
C.4
.

Lemma C.4
.

Under the same conditions as Theorem
5.5
, we have that
\|\mathbf{W}^{(T_{1})}-\mathbf{W}^{*}\|_{F}\leq\widetilde{O}(m^{3/2}\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{-1})+O(nm\rho_{0}(\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d})^{-1})
.

Lemma C.5
.

Under the same conditions as Theorem
5.5
, let
T=T_{1}+\Big\lfloor\frac{\|\mathbf{W}^{(T_{1})}-\mathbf{W}^{*}\|_{F}^{2}}{2\eta\epsilon}\Big\rfloor=T_{1}+\widetilde{O}(m^{3}\eta^{-1}\epsilon^{-1}\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{-2})
. Then we have
\max_{j,r,i}|\rho_{j,r,i}^{(t)}|\leq 4\rho_{0}
for all
T_{1}\leq t\leq T
. Besides,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{t-T_{1}+1}\sum_{s=T_{1}}^{t}L_{S}(\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\leq\frac{\|\mathbf{W}^{(T_{1})}-\mathbf{W}^{*}\|_{F}^{2}}{(2q-1)\eta(t-T_{1}+1)}+\frac{\epsilon}{2q-1}

for all
T_{1}\leq t\leq T
, and we can find an iteration with training loss smaller than
\epsilon
within
T
iterations.

C.1.3
Population Loss

In this section, the bound of the test loss is presented.
For a new data point
(\mathbf{x},y)
drawn from the same distribution as training data generated from. Without loss of generality,
we assume that the data point has the following structure: the first patch is the signal patch and the second patch is the noise patch, i.e.,
\mathbf{x}=[y\bm{\mu},\bm{\xi}]
.

Lemma C.6
.

Let
T
the same as defined in Lemma
C.5
in Second Stage (Section
C.1.2
). Under the same conditions as Theorem
5.5
, for any
0\leq t\leq T
with
L_{S}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\leq\frac{1}{4}
, it holds that
L_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\leq 6\cdot L_{S}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)})+\exp(-\widetilde{\Omega}(n^{2}))
.

Then, based on the above lemmas, we provide a simplified version of the proof for Theorem
5.5
.

Proof of Theorem
5.5
.

For the first result in Theorem
5.5
, based on the result of the pre-training stage in Theorem
5.3
, we have that the conditions
of Lemma
C.3
hold.
The result of First Stage signal learning in Lemma
C.3
hold. Then, we could define
\mathbf{W}^{*}
as (
C.13
), and by Lemma
C.4
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{W}^{(T_{1})}-\mathbf{W}^{*}\|_{F}\leq\widetilde{O}(m^{3/2}\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{-1})+O(nm\rho_{0}(\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d})^{-1}).

It follows that for any
\epsilon>0
, choose
T=T_{1}+\widetilde{O}(m^{3}\eta^{-1}\epsilon^{-1}\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{-2})
, by Lemma
C.5
,
we have that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T-T_{1}+1}\sum_{s=T_{1}}^{T}L_{S}(\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\leq\frac{\|\mathbf{W}^{(T_{1})}-\mathbf{W}^{*}\|_{F}^{2}}{(2q-1)\eta(T-T_{1}+1)}+\frac{\epsilon}{2q-1}\leq\frac{3\epsilon}{2q-1}<\epsilon.

Therefore, there exists some
T_{1}\leq t\leq T
with
L_{S}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\leq\epsilon
. This completes the proof of the first result.
Then combine this with Lemma
C.6
, the second result of Theorem
5.5
is given by

\displaystyle L_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\leq 6\cdot L_{S}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)})+\exp(-\widetilde{\Omega}(n^{2})).

∎

C.2
Proof of lemmas in Section
C.1

C.2.1
Proof of Lemma
C.3

To prove Lemma
C.3
, we first introduce the following Lemma
C.7
,
C.8
,
C.9
and
C.10
.

Lemma C.7
.

Suppose that
\delta>0
and
n\geq\Omega(\log(1/\delta))

Then with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle|\{i\in[n]:y_{i}=1\}|,~|\{i\in[n]:y_{i}=-1\}|\geq n/4.

The following Lemma
C.8
provides an estimate of the norm of
\bm{\xi}_{i}
and a bound of their inner products between each other.

Lemma C.8
.

Suppose that
\delta>0
and
d=\Omega(\log(4n/\delta))
. Then with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\sigma_{p}^{2}d/2\leq\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 3\sigma_{p}^{2}d/2,

\displaystyle|\langle\bm{\xi}_{i},\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}\rangle|\leq 2\sigma_{p}^{2}\cdot\sqrt{d\log(4n^{2}/\delta)},

for all
i,i^{\prime}\in[n]
.

Lemma C.9
.

Under Condition
4.1
, suppose (
C.16
), (
C.17
) and (
C.18
) hold at iteration
t
. Then

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y_{i}\bm{\mu}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\max\limits_{j,r}\{0,-\gamma_{j,r}^{(0)}\},

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq 32nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot\log(T^{\ast}),

for all
r\in[m]
and
j\not=y_{i}
.
Since by Assumption
C.1
,
\max\limits_{j,r}\{-\gamma_{j,r}^{(0)}\}\leq C_{0}
, we further have that
F_{j}(\mathbf{W}_{j}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i})=O(1)
.

Lemma C.10
.

Under Condition
4.1
, suppose (
C.16
), (
C.17
) and (
C.18
) hold at iteration
t
. Then

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y_{i}\bm{\mu}\rangle

\displaystyle=\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)},

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}+32nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot\log(T^{\ast})

for all
r\in[m]
,
j=y
and
i\in[n]
.
If
\max\limits_{j,r,i}\{\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)},\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}\}=O(1)
, we further have that
F_{j}(\mathbf{W}_{j}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i})=O(1)
.

Based on the above Lemma
C.7
,
C.8
,
C.9
and
C.10
, we could prove the Lemma
C.3
now.

Proof of Lemma
C.3
.

Let

\displaystyle T_{1}^{+}=\frac{1}{\frac{2\eta q}{nm}\sigma_{p}^{2}d\cdot 8^{q-1}\rho_{0}^{q-2}}.

(C.14)

We first prove the second conclusion (
C.12
).
Define
\Psi^{(t)}=\max_{j,r,i}|\rho_{j,r,i}^{(t)}|=\max_{j,r,i}\{\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)},-\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}\}
.
We use induction to show that

\displaystyle\Psi^{(t)}\leq 2\rho_{0}

(C.15)

for all
0\leq t\leq T_{1}^{+}
. By definition, clearly we have
\Psi^{(0)}=\rho_{0}
. Now suppose that there exists some
\widetilde{T}\leq T_{1}^{+}
such that (
C.15
) holds for
0<t\leq\widetilde{T}-1
.
Then by (
C.8
) we have

\displaystyle\Psi^{(t+1)}

\displaystyle\leq\Psi^{(t)}+\max_{j,r,i}\bigg\{\frac{\eta}{nm}\cdot|\ell_{i}^{\prime(t)}|\cdot\sigma^{\prime}\Bigg(\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}\Psi^{(t)}\cdot\frac{|\langle\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle|}{\|\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}\|_{2}^{2}}+\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}\Psi^{(t)}\cdot\frac{|\langle\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle|}{\|\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}\|_{2}^{2}}\Bigg)\cdot\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq\Psi^{(t)}+\max_{j,r,i}\bigg\{\frac{\eta}{nm}\cdot\sigma^{\prime}\Bigg(2\cdot\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}\Psi^{(t)}\cdot\frac{|\langle\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle|}{\|\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}\|_{2}^{2}}\Bigg)\cdot\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\bigg\}

\displaystyle=\Psi^{(t)}+\max_{j,r,i}\bigg\{\frac{\eta}{nm}\cdot\sigma^{\prime}\Bigg(2\Psi^{(t)}+2\cdot\sum_{i^{\prime}\neq i}^{n}\Psi^{(t)}\cdot\frac{|\langle\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle|}{\|\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}\|_{2}^{2}}\Bigg)\cdot\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq\Psi^{(t)}+\frac{\eta q}{nm}\cdot\Bigg[\Bigg(2+\frac{4n\sigma_{p}^{2}\cdot\sqrt{d\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}}{\sigma_{p}^{2}d/2}\Bigg)\cdot\Psi^{(t)}\Bigg]^{q-1}\cdot 2\sigma_{p}^{2}d

\displaystyle\leq\Psi^{(t)}+\frac{\eta q}{nm}\cdot\big(4\Psi^{(t)}\big)^{q-1}\cdot 2\sigma_{p}^{2}d

\displaystyle\leq\Psi^{(t)}+\frac{\eta q}{nm}\cdot\big(8\rho_{0}\big)^{q-1}\cdot 2\sigma_{p}^{2}d,

where the second inequality is by
|\ell_{i}^{\prime(t)}|\leq 1
,
the third inequality is due to Lemma
C.8
