, the fourth inequality follows by the condition that
d\geq 16n^{2}\log(4n^{2}/\delta)
in Condition
4.1
,
and the last inequality follows by the induction hypothesis (
C.15
). Taking a telescoping sum over
t=0,1,\ldots,\widetilde{T}-1
then gives

\displaystyle\Psi^{(\widetilde{T})}

\displaystyle\leq\Psi^{(0)}+\widetilde{T}\frac{\eta q}{nm}\cdot\big(8\rho_{0}\big)^{q-1}\cdot 2\sigma_{p}^{2}d

\displaystyle\leq\rho_{0}+T_{1}^{+}\frac{\eta q}{nm}\cdot\big(8\rho_{0}\big)^{q-1}\cdot 2\sigma_{p}^{2}d

\displaystyle\leq 2\rho_{0},

where the second inequality follows by
\widetilde{T}\leq T_{1}^{+}
in our induction hypothesis. Therefore, by induction, we prove that
\Psi^{(t)}\leq 2\rho_{0}
for all
t\leq T_{1}^{+}
.

To prove the first conclusion (
C.11
),
without loss of generality, consider
j=1
first (similar ideas for the proof of
j=-1
). Denote by
T_{1,1}

the last time for
t
in
[0,T_{1}^{+}]
satisfying that
\max_{r}\gamma_{1,r}^{(t)}\leq 2
. Then for
t\leq T_{1,1}
,

\max_{j,r,i}\{|\rho_{j,r,i}^{(t)}|\}=O(\rho_{0}\sigma_{p}^{2}d)=O(1)
and
\max_{r}\gamma_{1,r}^{(t)}\leq 2
.
Therefore, by Lemma
C.9
and
C.10
, we know that
F_{-1}(\mathbf{W}_{-1}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i}),F_{+1}(\mathbf{W}_{+1}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i})=O(1)
for all
i
with
y_{i}=1
. Thus, there exists a positive constant
C_{1}
such that
-\ell^{\prime(t)}_{i}\geq C_{1}
for all
i
with
y_{i}=1
.

Since (
C.11
) focuses on the
\max_{r}\gamma_{1,r}^{(t)}
, we only need to consider the training dynamic of
\max_{r}\gamma_{1,r}^{(t)}
, which is positive at time
t=0
by Assumption
C.2
.
By (
C.8
), for positive
\gamma_{1,r}^{(t)}
and
t\leq T_{1,1}
we have

\displaystyle\gamma_{1,r}^{(t+1)}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{1,r}^{(t)}-\frac{\eta}{nm}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell_{i}^{\prime(t)}\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(y_{i}\cdot\gamma_{1,r}^{(t)})\cdot\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\gamma_{1,r}^{(t)}+\frac{C_{1}\eta}{nm}\cdot\sum_{y_{i}=1}\sigma^{\prime}(\gamma_{1,r}^{(t)})\cdot\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}.

Denote
A^{(t)}=\max_{r}\gamma_{1,r}^{(t)}
,
\gamma_{0}
is defined in Assumption
C.2
with
\max_{r}\gamma_{1,r}^{(0)}\geq\gamma_{0}\geq 0
.
Then we have

\displaystyle A^{(t+1)}

\displaystyle\geq A^{(t)}+\frac{C_{1}\eta}{nm}\cdot\sum_{y_{i}=1}\sigma^{\prime}(A^{(t)})\cdot\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq A^{(t)}+\frac{C_{1}\eta q\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}}{4m}\big(A^{(t)}\big)^{q-1}

\displaystyle\geq\bigg[1+\frac{C_{1}\eta q\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}}{4m}\big(A^{(0)}\big)^{q-2}\bigg]A^{(t)}

\displaystyle\geq\bigg(1+\frac{C_{1}\eta q\gamma_{0}^{q-2}\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}}{4m}\bigg)A^{(t)},

where the second inequality is by the lower bound on the number of positive data in Lemma
C.7

, the third inequality is due to the fact that
A^{(t)}
is an increasing sequence, and the last inequality follows by

A^{(0)}=\max_{r}\langle\mathbf{w}_{1,r}^{(0)},\bm{\mu}\rangle\geq\gamma_{0}
. Therefore, the sequence
A^{(t)}
will exponentially grow and we have that

\displaystyle A^{(t)}

\displaystyle\geq A^{(0)}\bigg(1+\frac{C_{1}\eta q\gamma_{0}^{q-2}\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}}{4m}\bigg)^{t}\geq A^{(0)}\exp\bigg(\frac{C_{1}\eta q\gamma_{0}^{q-2}\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}}{8m}t\bigg)\geq\gamma_{0}\exp\bigg(\frac{C_{1}\eta q\gamma_{0}^{q-2}\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}}{8m}t\bigg),

where the second inequality is due to the fact that
1+z\geq\exp(z/2)
for
z\leq 2
and our condition of
\eta\leq O(mq^{-1}\gamma_{0}^{-(q-2)}\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{-2})
in Condition
4.1
, and the last inequality follows by
A^{(0)}=\max_{r}\gamma_{1,r}^{(0)}
.
Therefore,
A^{(t)}=\max_{r}\gamma_{1,r}^{(t)}
will reach
2
within

T_{1}=\frac{\log(2/\gamma_{0})8m}{C_{1}\eta q\gamma_{0}^{q-2}\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}}

iterations.

We can next verify the value of
T_{1}
and
T_{1}^{+}
follow the following relationship

\displaystyle T_{1}=\frac{\log(2/\gamma_{0})8m}{C_{1}\eta q\gamma_{0}^{q-2}\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{\frac{4\eta q}{nm}\sigma_{p}^{2}d\cdot 8^{q-1}\rho_{0}^{q-2}}=T_{1}^{+}/2,

where the inequality holds due to our SNR condition in (
C.10
). Therefore, by the definition of
T_{1,1}
, we have
T_{1,1}\leq T_{1}\leq T_{1}^{+}/2
, where we use the non-decreasing property of
\gamma
. The proof for
j=-1
is similar, and we can prove that
\max_{r}\gamma_{-1,r}^{(T_{1,-1})}\geq 2
while
T_{1,-1}\leq T_{1}\leq T_{1}^{+}/2
, which completes the proof.
∎

C.2.2
Proof of Lemma
C.4

Before proving the Lemma
C.4
, we first show the following Proposition
C.11
, which shows that
the coefficients
\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)},\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)},\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}
will stay a reasonable scale during the training period
0<t<T^{*}
.

Proposition C.11
.

Under Condition
4.1
, which indicates that
16n\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\leq 0.5
, if Assumption
C.1
holds, then
for
0\leq t\leq T^{*}
, we have that

\displaystyle-4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{\ast})\leq\gamma_{j,r}^{(0)}\leq\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)}\leq 4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{\ast}),

(C.16)

\displaystyle 0\leq\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}\leq 4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{\ast}),

(C.17)

\displaystyle 0\geq\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}\geq-64nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot\log(T^{\ast})\geq-4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{\ast}),

(C.18)

for all
r\in[m]
,
j\in\{\pm 1\}
and
i\in[n]
.

Then, based on Proposition
C.11
and Lemma
C.3
, we could prove the following Lemma
C.4
.

Proof of Lemma
C.4
.

We have

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{W}^{(T_{1})}-\mathbf{W}^{*}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j,r}\frac{|\gamma_{j,r}^{(T_{1})}|}{\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}}+\sum_{j,r,i}\frac{|\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(T_{1})}|}{\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}}+\sum_{j,r,i}\frac{|\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(T_{1})}|}{\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}}+O(m^{3/2}\log(1/\epsilon))\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq O(m\|\bm{\mu}\|^{-1})+O(nm\rho_{0}(\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d})^{-1})+O(m^{3/2}\log(1/\epsilon))\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{O}(m^{3/2}\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{-1})+O(nm\rho_{0}(\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d})^{-1}),

where the first inequality is by our decomposition of
\mathbf{W}^{(T_{1})}
and the definition of
\mathbf{W}^{*}
,
the second inequality is by Proposition
C.11
and Lemma
C.3
.
∎

C.2.3
Proof of Lemma
C.5

In this section, Lemma
C.12
is presented first, then Lemma
C.13
and
C.14
are proved before finally proving Lemma
C.5
.
Based on Proposition
C.11
, the following Lemma
C.12
introduces some important properties of the training loss function for
0\leq t\leq T^{*}
.

Lemma C.12
.

Under Condition
4.1
, for
0\leq t\leq T^{*}
, the following result holds,

\displaystyle\|\nabla L_{S}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\|_{F}^{2}\leq O(\max\{\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2},\sigma_{p}^{2}d\})L_{S}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)}).

Lemma C.13
.

Under the same conditions as Theorem
5.5
, we have that

y_{i}\langle\nabla f(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i}),\mathbf{W}^{*}\rangle\geq q^{2}2^{q}\log(2q/\epsilon)
for all
i\in[n]
and
T_{1}\leq t\leq T^{*}
.

Proof of Lemma
C.13
.

Recall that
f(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i})=(1/m){\sum_{j,r}}j\cdot\big[\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y_{i}\cdot\bm{\mu}\rangle)+\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle)\big]
and the definition of
\mathbf{W}^{*}
in (
C.13
),
we have

\displaystyle y_{i}\langle\nabla f(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i}),\mathbf{W}^{*}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j,r}\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y_{i}\bm{\mu}\rangle)\langle\bm{\mu},j\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{*}\rangle+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j,r}\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle)\langle y_{i}\bm{\xi}_{i},j\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{*}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j,r}\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y_{i}\bm{\mu}\rangle)2qm\log(2q/\epsilon)

(C.19)

where the second equality holds because
\langle\bm{\mu},j\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{*}\rangle=2qm\log(2q/\epsilon)
,
\langle y_{i}\bm{\xi}_{i},j\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{*}\rangle=0
by
\langle\bm{\mu},j\mathbf{w}_{jr}^{\perp}\rangle=0
,
\langle y_{i}\bm{\xi}_{i},j\mathbf{w}_{jr}^{\perp}\rangle=0
in the definition of
\mathbf{W}^{*}
(
C.13
).

Next we will give a bound for the inner-product term in (
C.19
). By Lemma
C.10
and the initialization and non-decreasing property of
\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)}
in Second Stage (Section
C.1.2
), we have that for
j=y_{i}

\displaystyle\max_{r}\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y_{i}\bm{\mu}\rangle=\max_{r}\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)}\geq 2.

(C.20)

Plug (
C.20
) into (
C.19
) can we obtain

\displaystyle y_{i}\langle\nabla f(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i}),\mathbf{W}^{*}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq q^{2}2^{q}\log(2q/\epsilon)

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma C.14
.

Under the same conditions as Theorem
5.5
, we have that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{W}^{(t)}-\mathbf{W}^{*}\|_{F}^{2}-\|\mathbf{W}^{(t+1)}-\mathbf{W}^{*}\|_{F}^{2}\geq(2q-1)\eta L_{S}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)})-\eta\epsilon

for all
T_{1}\leq t\leq T^{*}
.

Proof of Lemma
C.14
.

Here we assume the neural network is
q
homogeneous, namely
\langle\nabla f(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i}),\mathbf{W}^{(t)}\rangle=qf(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i})
, thus we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{W}^{(t)}-\mathbf{W}^{*}\|_{F}^{2}-\|\mathbf{W}^{(t+1)}-\mathbf{W}^{*}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=2\eta\langle\nabla L_{S}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)}),\mathbf{W}^{(t)}-\mathbf{W}^{*}\rangle-\eta^{2}\|\nabla L_{S}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\eta}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell^{\prime(t)}_{i}[qy_{i}f(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i})-y_{i}\langle\nabla f(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i}),\mathbf{W}^{*}\rangle]-\eta^{2}\|\nabla L_{S}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{2\eta}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell^{\prime(t)}_{i}[qy_{i}f(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i})-q^{2}2^{q}\log(2q/\epsilon)]-\eta^{2}\|\nabla L_{S}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{2q\eta}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}[\ell\big(y_{i}f(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i})\big)-\ell(q2^{q}\log(2q/\epsilon))]-\eta^{2}\|\nabla L_{S}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{2q\eta}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}[\ell\big(y_{i}f(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i})\big)-\epsilon/(2q)]-\eta^{2}\|\nabla L_{S}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq(2q-1)\eta L_{S}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)})-\eta\epsilon,

where the first inequality is by Lemma
C.13
,
the second and third inequality is due to the convexity of the cross entropy function and the property of loss function,
and the last inequality is by Lemma
C.12
and by
\eta\leq O\Big(\min\{\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{-2},(\sigma_{p}^{2}\sqrt{d})^{-2}\}\Big)
in Condition
4.1
.
∎

Based on the above lemmas,
the proof of Lemma
C.5
is presented as follows.

Proof of Lemma
C.5
.

By Lemma
C.14
, for any
t\in[T_{1},T]
, we have that for
s\leq t

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{W}^{(s)}-\mathbf{W}^{*}\|_{F}^{2}-\|\mathbf{W}^{(s+1)}-\mathbf{W}^{*}\|_{F}^{2}\geq(2q-1)\eta L_{S}(\mathbf{W}^{(s)})-\eta\epsilon

holds.
Taking a summation, we obtain that

\displaystyle\sum_{s=T_{1}}^{t}L_{S}(\mathbf{W}^{(s)})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|\mathbf{W}^{(T_{1})}-\mathbf{W}^{*}\|_{F}^{2}+\eta\epsilon(t-T_{1}+1)}{(2q-1)\eta}

(C.21)

for all
T_{1}\leq t\leq T
.
Dividing
(t-T_{1}+1)
on both side of (
C.21
) gives that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{t-T_{1}+1}\sum_{s=T_{1}}^{t}L_{S}(\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\leq\frac{\|\mathbf{W}^{(T_{1})}-\mathbf{W}^{*}\|_{F}^{2}}{(2q-1)\eta(t-T_{1}+1)}+\frac{\epsilon}{2q-1}.

Then we can take
t=T
where
T=T_{1}+\Big\lfloor\frac{\|\mathbf{W}^{(T_{1})}-\mathbf{W}^{*}\|_{F}^{2}}{2\eta\epsilon}\Big\rfloor
and have that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T-T_{1}+1}\sum_{s=T_{1}}^{T}L_{S}(\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\leq\frac{\|\mathbf{W}^{(T_{1})}-\mathbf{W}^{*}\|_{F}^{2}}{(2q-1)\eta(T-T_{1}+1)}+\frac{\epsilon}{2q-1}\leq\frac{3\epsilon}{2q-1}<\epsilon,

where we use the fact that
q>2
and the choice of
T
. Since the mean is smaller than
\epsilon
, we can conclude that there exist
T_{1}\leq t\leq T
such that
L_{S}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)})<\epsilon
.

Secondly, we will prove that
\max_{j,r,i}|\rho_{j,r,i}^{(t)}|\leq 4\rho_{0}
for all
t\in[T_{1},T]
.
Plugging
T=T_{1}+\Big\lfloor\frac{\|\mathbf{W}^{(T_{1})}-\mathbf{W}^{*}\|_{F}^{2}}{2\eta\epsilon}\Big\rfloor
into (
C.21
) gives that

\displaystyle\sum_{s=T_{1}}^{T}L_{S}(\mathbf{W}^{(s)})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\|\mathbf{W}^{(T_{1})}-\mathbf{W}^{*}\|_{F}^{2}}{(2q-1)\eta}=\widetilde{O}(\eta^{-1}m^{3}\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{-2})+O(\eta^{-1}n^{2}m^{2}{\rho_{0}}^{2}(\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d})^{-2}),

(C.22)

where the inequality is due to
\|\mathbf{W}^{(T_{1})}-\mathbf{W}^{*}\|_{F}\leq\widetilde{O}(m^{3/2}\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{-1})+O(nm\rho_{0}(\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d})^{-1})
in Lemma
C.4
.
Define
\Psi^{(t)}=\max_{j,r,i}|\rho_{j,r,i}^{(t)}|
.
We will use induction to prove
\Psi^{(t)}\leq 4\rho_{0}
for all
t\in[T_{1},T]
. At
t=T_{1}
, by the properties at the beginning of Second Stage (Section
C.1.2
),
we have
\Psi^{(T_{1})}\leq 2\rho_{0}
.
Now suppose that there exists
\widetilde{T}\in[T_{1},T]
such that
\Psi^{(t)}\leq 4\rho_{0}
for all
t\in[T_{1},\widetilde{T}-1]
.
Then we prove it also holds for
t=\widetilde{T}
:
For
t\in[T_{1},\widetilde{T}-1]
, by (
C.8
), we have

\displaystyle\Psi^{(t+1)}

\displaystyle\leq\Psi^{(t)}+\max_{j,r,i}\bigg\{\frac{\eta}{nm}\cdot|\ell_{i}^{\prime(t)}|\cdot\sigma^{\prime}\Bigg(2\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}\Psi^{(t)}\cdot\frac{|\langle\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle|}{\|\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}\|_{2}^{2}}\Bigg)\cdot\|\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}\|_{2}^{2}\bigg\}

\displaystyle=\Psi^{(t)}+\max_{j,r,i}\bigg\{\frac{\eta}{nm}\cdot|\ell_{i}^{\prime(t)}|\cdot\sigma^{\prime}\Bigg(2\Psi^{(t)}+2\sum_{i^{\prime}\neq i}^{n}\Psi^{(t)}\cdot\frac{|\langle\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle|}{\|\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}\|_{2}^{2}}\Bigg)\cdot\|\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}\|_{2}^{2}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq\Psi^{(t)}+\frac{\eta q}{nm}\cdot\max_{i}|\ell_{i}^{\prime(t)}|\cdot\Bigg[\Bigg(2+\frac{4n\sigma_{p}^{2}\cdot\sqrt{d\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}}{\sigma_{p}^{2}d/2}\Bigg)\cdot\Psi^{(t)}\Bigg]^{q-1}\cdot 2\sigma_{p}^{2}d

\displaystyle\leq\Psi^{(t)}+\frac{\eta q}{nm}\cdot\max_{i}|\ell_{i}^{\prime(t)}|\cdot\big(4\cdot\Psi^{(t)}\big)^{q-1}\cdot 2\sigma_{p}^{2}d,

where the second inequality is due to Lemma
C.8
,
and the last inequality follows by the assumption that
d\geq 1024n^{2}\log(4n^{2}/\delta)
in Condition
4.1
.
Taking a telescoping sum over
t=T_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{T}-1
, we have that

\displaystyle\Psi^{(T)}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}\Psi^{(T_{1})}+\frac{\eta q}{nm}\sum_{s=T_{1}}^{\widetilde{T}-1}\max_{i}|\ell_{i}^{\prime(s)}|\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{p}^{2}d)\cdot(2\rho_{0})^{q-1}

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\leq}\Psi^{(T_{1})}+\frac{\eta q}{nm}4^{q-1}2^{q}\sigma_{p}^{2}d(2\rho_{0})^{q-1}\sum_{s=T_{1}}^{\widetilde{T}-1}\max_{i}\ell_{i}^{(s)}

\displaystyle\overset{(iii)}{\leq}\Psi^{(T_{1})}+\eta qm^{-1}4^{q-1}2^{q}\sigma_{p}^{2}d(2\rho_{0})^{q-1}\sum_{s=T_{1}}^{\widetilde{T}-1}L_{S}(\mathbf{W}^{(s)})

\displaystyle\overset{(iv)}{\leq}\Psi^{(T_{1})}+\widetilde{O}(qm^{2}4^{q-1}2^{q}\mathrm{SNR}^{-2})\cdot(2\rho_{0})^{q-1}

\displaystyle\leq 2\rho_{0}+\widetilde{O}(qm^{2}4^{q-1}2^{q}(2\rho_{0})^{q-2}\mathrm{SNR}^{-2})\cdot 2\rho_{0}

\displaystyle\overset{(v)}{\leq}2\rho_{0}+\rho_{0}+\rho_{0}

\displaystyle=4\rho_{0},

where (i) is by out induction hypothesis that
\Psi^{(t)}\leq 4\rho_{0}
for
t\in[T_{1},\widetilde{T}-1]
,
(ii) is by
|\ell^{\prime}|\leq\ell
,
(iii) is by
\max_{i}\ell_{i}^{(s)}\leq\sum_{i}\ell_{i}^{(s)}=nL_{S}(\mathbf{W}^{(s)})
,
(iv) is due to
\sum_{s=T_{1}}^{\widetilde{T}-1}L_{S}(\mathbf{W}^{(s)})\leq\sum_{s=T_{1}}^{T}L_{S}(\mathbf{W}^{(s)})=\widetilde{O}(\eta^{-1}m^{3}\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{-2})+O(\eta^{-1}n^{2}m^{2}{\rho_{0}}^{2}(\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d})^{-2})
in (
C.22
),
(v) is by the condition for
\mathrm{SNR}:

\mathrm{SNR}^{2}\geq\widetilde{\Omega}(2qm^{2}4^{q-1}2^{q}(2\rho_{0})^{q-2})=\widetilde{\Omega}(2qm^{2}16^{q-1}\rho_{0}^{q-2})

and
\rho_{0}\leq O((\frac{1}{8}qn^{2}m)^{-\frac{1}{q}})
obtained from Theorem
5.3
.
This completes the induction.
∎

C.2.4
Proof of Lemma
C.6

We first present the following Lemma
C.15
, which shows the bound of
\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle
.

Lemma C.15
.

Under Condition
4.1
, suppose (
C.16
), (
C.17
) and (
C.18
) hold at iteration
t
. Then

\displaystyle\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}-32nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot\log(T^{\ast})\leq\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}+32nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot\log(T^{\ast}),~j\not=y_{i},

\displaystyle\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}-32nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot\log(T^{\ast})\leq\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}+32nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot\log(T^{\ast}),~j=y_{i}

for all
r\in[m]
,
j\in\{\pm 1\}
and
i\in[n]
.

We then prove the following two lemmas before proving Lemma
C.6
.

Lemma C.16
.

Under the same conditions as Theorem
5.5
, we have that
\max_{j,r}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle|\leq 1/2
for all
0\leq t\leq T
, where
T

is defined in Lemma
C.5
in Second Stage (Section
C.1.2
).

Proof of Lemma
C.16
.

We can get the upper bound of the inner products between the parameter and the noise as follows:

\displaystyle|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle|

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}|\rho_{j,r,i}^{(t)}|+8n\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot 4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*})

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\leq}4\rho_{0}+8n\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot 4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*})

\displaystyle\overset{(iii)}{\leq}1/2

for all
j\in\{\pm 1\}
,
r\in[m]
and
i\in[n]
,
where (i) is by Lemma
C.15
,
(ii) is by
\max_{j,r,i}|\rho_{j,r,i}^{(t)}|\leq 4\rho_{0}
in Lemma
C.5
,
and (iii) is due to the condition
8n\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot 4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*})\leq 1/4
in Condition
4.1
and the result

\rho_{0}\leq 1/16
in Theorem
5.3
.
∎

The following Lemma
C.17
provides the upper bound for
\max_{j,r}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}\rangle|
, where
\bm{\xi}
is from the test population.

Lemma C.17
.

Under the same conditions as Theorem
5.5
, with probability at least
1-4mT\cdot\exp(-\Omega(n^{2}))
, we have that
\max_{j,r}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}\rangle|\leq 1/2
for all
0\leq t\leq T
.

Proof of Lemma
C.17
.

Define
\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j,r}^{(t)}=\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)}-j\cdot\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)}\cdot\frac{\bm{\mu}}{\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}}
, then we have
\langle\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}\rangle=\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}\rangle
.
Since
\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j,r}^{(t)}=\mathbf{w}_{jr}^{\perp}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\rho_{j,r,i}^{(t)}\cdot\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{-2}\cdot\bm{\xi}_{i}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j,r}^{(t)}\|_{2}\leq\|\mathbf{w}_{jr}^{\perp}\|_{2}+4n\rho_{0}\frac{2}{\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d}}=\|\mathbf{w}_{jr}^{\perp}\|_{2}+\widetilde{O}(\frac{n\rho_{0}}{\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d}}),

(C.23)

where the inequality is due to the bound for
\rho_{j,r,i}^{(t)}
and
\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}
.

By (
C.23
),
\max_{j,r}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j,r}^{(t)}\|_{2}\leq\max_{j,r}\|\mathbf{w}_{jr}^{\perp}\|_{2}+\widetilde{C}_{2}\frac{n\rho_{0}}{\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d}}
, where
\widetilde{C}_{2}=\widetilde{O}(1)
.
Clearly
\langle\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}\rangle
is a Gaussian distribution with mean zero and standard deviation smaller than
\max_{j,r}\|\mathbf{w}_{jr}^{\perp}\|_{2}+\widetilde{C}_{2}\frac{n\rho_{0}}{\sqrt{d}}
. Therefore, the probability is bounded by

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(|\langle\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}\rangle|\geq 1/2\big)\leq

\displaystyle 2\exp\bigg(-\frac{1}{8\big[\frac{\widetilde{C}_{2}^{2}n^{2}{\rho_{0}}^{2}}{d}+2\frac{\widetilde{C}_{2}n\rho_{0}}{\sqrt{d}}\max_{j,r}\|\mathbf{w}_{jr}^{\perp}\|_{2}+(\max_{j,r}\|\mathbf{w}_{jr}^{\perp}\|_{2})^{2}\big]}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\exp\bigg(-\frac{1}{8\big[\frac{\widetilde{C}_{2}^{2}n^{2}{\rho_{0}}^{2}}{d}+2\frac{\widetilde{C}_{2}\rho_{0}}{\sqrt{d}}+n^{-2}\big]}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\exp\big(-\Omega\big(n^{2}\big)\big),

where the second inequality is by the assumption
\max_{j,r}\|\mathbf{w}_{jr}^{\perp}\|_{2}\leq 1/n
, the third inequality is by
\rho_{0}\leq O(\sqrt{d}/n^{2})
in Theorem
5.3
.
Applying a union bound over
j,r,t
completes the proof.
∎

Based on Lemmas
C.16
and
C.17
, we now prove Lemma
C.6
.

Proof of Lemma
C.6
.

Let event
\mathcal{E}
to be the event that Lemma
C.17
holds. Then we can divide
L_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)})
into two parts:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\ell\big(yf(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x})\big)\big]

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\mathbb{E}[\ind(\mathcal{E})\ell\big(yf(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x})\big)]}_{I_{1}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}[\ind(\mathcal{E}^{c})\ell\big(yf(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x})\big)]}_{I_{2}}.

(C.24)

In the following analysis, we bound
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
respectively.

Bounding
I_{1}
:

Denote
I_{j}=\{i|y_{i}=j\},~j=\pm 1
.
Since we have

L_{S}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)})=\frac{1}{n}\left[\sum_{i^{\prime}\in I_{+}}\ell\big(y_{i^{\prime}}f(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i^{\prime}})\big)+\sum_{i^{\prime}\in I_{-}}\ell\big(y_{i^{\prime}}f(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i^{\prime}})\big)\right]\leq\frac{1}{4},

thus,
\sum_{i^{\prime}\in I_{j}}\ell\big(y_{i^{\prime}}f(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i^{\prime}})\big)\leq\frac{1}{4},~j=\pm 1
.
It follows that for
j=\pm 1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|I_{j}|}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in I_{j}}\ell\big(y_{i^{\prime}}f(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i^{\prime}})\big)\leq\frac{n}{|I_{j}|}\frac{1}{4}\leq 1

where the last inequality is by Lemma
C.7
.
Therefore, there must exist one
(\mathbf{x}_{i},y_{i})
with
y=y_{i}\in I_{j_{0}}
such that

\ell\big(y_{i}f(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i})\big)\leq\frac{1}{|I_{j_{0}}|}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in I_{j_{0}}}\ell\big(y_{i^{\prime}}f(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i^{\prime}})\big)\leq 1
, which implies that
y_{i}f(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i})\geq 0
.
Therefore, we have that

\displaystyle\exp(-y_{i}f(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i}))\overset{(i)}{\leq}2\log\big(1+\exp(-y_{i}f(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i}))\big)=2\ell\big(y_{i}f(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i})\big)\leq 2L_{S}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)}),

(C.25)

where (i) is by
z\leq 2\log(1+z),~\text{for}~z\leq 1
and here we have
\exp(-y_{i}f(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i}))\leq 1
.
If event
\mathcal{E}
holds, we have that

\displaystyle|yf(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x})-y_{i}f(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i})|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j,r}\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle)+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j,r}\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}\rangle)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j,r}\sigma(1/2)+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j,r}\sigma(1/2)

\displaystyle\leq 1,

(C.26)

where the second inequality is by
\max_{j,r}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}\rangle|\leq 1/2
in Lemma
C.17
and
\max_{j,r}|\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle|\leq 1/2
in Lemma
C.16
.
Thus, we have that

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}[\ind(\mathcal{E})\exp(-yf(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}))]

\displaystyle\leq e\cdot\mathbb{E}[\ind(\mathcal{E})\exp(-y_{i}f(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i}))]

\displaystyle\leq 2e\cdot\mathbb{E}[\ind(\mathcal{E})L_{S}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)})],

where the first inequality is by the property of cross-entropy loss that
\ell(z)\leq\exp(-z)
for all
z
,
the second inequality is by
-yf(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x})\leq 1-y_{i}f(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i})
in (
C.26
),
and the third inequality is by
\exp(-y_{i}f(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i}))\leq 2L_{S}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)})
in (
C.25
). Dropping the event in the expectation gives
I_{1}\leq 6L_{S}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)})
.

Bounding
I_{2}
:
Next we bound the second term
I_{2}
. We choose an arbitrary training data
(\mathbf{x}_{i^{\prime}},y_{i^{\prime}})
such that
y_{i^{\prime}}=y
. Then we have

\displaystyle\ell\big(yf(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x})\big)

\displaystyle=\log(1+\exp(-yF_{+}(\mathbf{W}_{+}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}))+yF_{-}(\mathbf{W}_{-}^{(t)},\mathbf{x})))

\displaystyle\leq\log(1+\exp(F_{-y}(\mathbf{W}_{-y}^{(t)},\mathbf{x})))

\displaystyle\leq 1+F_{-y}(\mathbf{W}_{-y}^{(t)},\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=1+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=-y,r\in[m]}\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y\bm{\mu}\rangle)+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=-y,r\in[m]}\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}\rangle)

\displaystyle\leq 1+4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*})+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=-y,r\in[m]}\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}\rangle)

\displaystyle\leq 1+4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*})+\widetilde{O}((n\rho_{0}\sigma_{p}^{-1}d^{-\frac{1}{2}})^{q}))\|\bm{\xi}\|_{2}^{q},

(C.27)

where the first inequality is due to
F_{y}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x})\geq 0
,
the second inequality is by the property of cross-entropy loss, i.e.,
\log(1+\exp(z))\leq 1+z
for all
z\geq 0
,
the third inequality is by Lemma
C.9
and (
C.9
) in Assumption
C.2
, i.e.,

\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=-y,r\in[m]}\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y\bm{\mu}\rangle)\leq\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=-y,r\in[m]}\sigma(-\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)})\leq\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=-y,r\in[m]}\sigma(-\gamma_{j,r}^{(0)})\leq\max_{j,r}\{0,(-\gamma^{(0)}_{j,r})^{q}\}\ll 4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*})
,
and the last inequality is by (
C.23
), we have
\langle\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}\rangle=\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}\rangle\leq\|\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j,r}^{(t)}\|_{2}\cdot\|\bm{\xi}\|_{2}\leq\widetilde{O}(n\rho_{0}\sigma_{p}^{-1}d^{-\frac{1}{2}})\|\bm{\xi}\|_{2}
. Then we further have that

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\ind(\mathcal{E}^{c})]}\cdot\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\Big[\ell\big(yf(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x})\big)^{2}\Big]}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}^{c})}\cdot\sqrt{[1+4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*})]^{2}+\widetilde{O}(n^{2q}{\rho_{0}}^{2q}\sigma_{p}^{-2q}d^{-q})\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{\xi}\|_{2}^{2q}]}

\displaystyle\leq\exp[-\widetilde{\Omega}(n^{2})+\text{polylog}(n)]

\displaystyle\leq\exp(-\widetilde{\Omega}(n^{2})),

where the first inequality is by Cauchy-Schwartz inequality,
the second inequality is by (
C.27
),
the third inequality is by Lemma
C.17
, the definition of
\bm{\xi}
, and the result
\rho_{0}\leq 1/16
in Theorem
5.3
.

Plugging the bounds of
I_{1}
,
I_{2}
into (
C.24
) completes the proof.
∎

C.3
Proof of lemmas in Section
C.2

In this section, we prove the lemmas used in the proof of Section
C.2
. These lemmas are mainly concerned with the properties of data and the basic properties of the coefficients
\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)},\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)},\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}
.

We first prove the following Lemmas
C.7
and
C.8
, which are related to the data distribution.

Proof of Lemma
C.7
.

Since
y_{i}
follow Rademache distribution, then by Hoeffding’s inequality, with probability at least
1-\delta/2
,

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ind\{y_{i}=1\}-\frac{n}{2}\Big|\leq\sqrt{2n\log(4/\delta)}.

By our assumption
n\geq\Omega(\log(1/\delta))
, it follows that

\displaystyle|\{i\in[n]:y_{i}=1\}|=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ind\{y_{i}=1\}\geq\frac{n}{2}-\sqrt{2n\log(4/\delta)}\geq\frac{n}{4}.

Same result could be obtained for
|\{i\in[n]:y_{i}=-1\}|
.
Apply a union bound finishes the proof of this lemma.
∎

Proof of Lemma
C.8
.

Since
\bm{\xi}_{i},i\in[n]
i.i.d follows
N(\mathbf{0},\sigma_{p}^{2}\cdot(\mathbf{I}-\bm{\mu}\bm{\mu}^{\top}\cdot\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{-2}))
,
the proof follows exactly same proof as Lemma
B.1
. By Bernstein inequality, with the probability of at least
1-\delta/(2n)
, we have that

\displaystyle d\sigma_{p}^{2}-C\sigma_{p}^{2}\sqrt{d\log(4/\delta})\leq\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\leq d\sigma_{p}^{2}+C\sigma_{p}^{2}\sqrt{d\log(4n/\delta}),

where
C
is an absolute constant that does not depend on other variables. By assumption
d=\Omega(\log(4n/\delta))
, it follows that

\displaystyle\sigma_{p}^{2}d/2\leq\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 3\sigma_{p}^{2}d/2

For the second result, by Bernstein inequality, for all
i,i^{\prime}\in[n]
with
i\neq i^{\prime}
, with the probability of at least
1-\delta/(2n^{2})
, we have that

\displaystyle|\langle\bm{\xi}_{i},\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}\rangle|\leq 2\sigma_{p}^{2}\cdot\sqrt{d\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}.

Apply a union bound for finishes the proof of this lemma.
∎

We then prove a series of lemmas that will be used in the proof of Proposition
C.11
by induction.

Lemma C.18
.

For any
t\geq 0
, it holds that
\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\mu}\rangle=j\cdot\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)}
for all
r\in[m]
,
j\in\{\pm 1\}
.

Proof of Lemma
C.18
.

For any
t\geq 0
, we have that

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\mu}\rangle

\displaystyle=j\cdot\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)}+\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i^{\prime}}^{(t)}\|\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}\|_{2}^{-2}\cdot\langle\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}},\bm{\mu}\rangle+\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i^{\prime}}^{(t)}\|\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}\|_{2}^{-2}\cdot\langle\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}},\bm{\mu}\rangle

\displaystyle=j\cdot\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)},

where the equality is by our orthogonal assumption of
\bm{\xi}
and
\bm{\mu}
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
C.15
.

For
j\not=y_{i}
, we have
\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}=0
and

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i^{\prime}}^{(t)}\|\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}\|_{2}^{-2}\cdot\langle\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle+\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i^{\prime}}^{(t)}\|\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}\|_{2}^{-2}\cdot\langle\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq 4\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\sum_{i^{\prime}\not=i}|\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i^{\prime}}^{(t)}|+4\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\sum_{i^{\prime}\not=i}|\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i^{\prime}}^{(t)}|+\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\leq\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}+32nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot\log(T^{\ast}),

where the second inequality is by Lemma
C.8
and the last inequality is by
|\overline{\rho}^{(t)}_{j,r,i^{\prime}}|,|\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i^{\prime}}^{(t)}|\leq 4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{\ast})
in (
C.17
).

For
y_{i}=j
, we have that
\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}=0
and

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i^{\prime}}^{(t)}\|\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}\|_{2}^{-2}\cdot\langle\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle+\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i^{\prime}}^{(t)}\|\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}}\|_{2}^{-2}\cdot\langle\bm{\xi}_{i^{\prime}},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}+4\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\sum_{i^{\prime}\not=i}|\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i^{\prime}}^{(t)}|+4\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\sum_{i^{\prime}\not=i}|\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i^{\prime}}^{(t)}|

\displaystyle\leq\overline{\rho}^{(t)}_{j,r,i}+32nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\log(T^{\ast}),

where the first inequality is by Lemma
C.7
and the second inequality is by
|\overline{\rho}^{(t)}_{j,r,i^{\prime}}|,|\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i^{\prime}}^{(t)}|\leq 4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{\ast})
in (
C.17
).
Similarly, we can show that
\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\geq\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}-32nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)/d}\cdot\log(T^{\ast})
and
\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\geq\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}-32nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)/d}\cdot\log(T^{\ast})
.
This completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
C.9
.

For
j\not=y_{i}
, by Lemma
C.18
, we have that

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y_{i}\bm{\mu}\rangle=y_{i}\cdot j\cdot\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)}=-\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)}\leq\left\{\begin{aligned} &0,~\text{if}~\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)}\geq 0\\
&-\gamma_{j,r}^{(0)},~\text{otherwise}\\
\end{aligned}\right..

(C.28)

Also, we have

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\leq\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}+32nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot\log(T^{\ast})\leq 32nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot\log(T^{\ast}),

(C.29)

where the first inequality is by Lemma
C.15
and the second inequality is due to
\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}\leq 0
.
Thus, we can get that

\displaystyle F_{j}(\mathbf{W}_{j}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r=1}^{m}[\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},-j\cdot\bm{\mu}\rangle)+\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle)]

\displaystyle\leq 2\cdot 2^{q}\max\limits_{j,r}\bigg\{-\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)},32nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot\log(T^{\ast})\bigg\}^{q}

\displaystyle=O(1),

where the first inequality is by (
C.28
), (
C.29
) and the last line is by Condition
4.1
which implies
128nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot\log(T^{\ast})\leq 1
and

-\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)}\leq\max\limits_{j,r}\{0,-\gamma_{j,r}^{(0)}\}\leq C_{0}

in Assumption
C.1
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
C.10
.

For
j=y_{i}
, we have that

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y_{i}\bm{\mu}\rangle=\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)},

(C.30)

where the equality is by Lemma
C.18
. We also have that

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\leq\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}+32nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot\log(T^{\ast}),

(C.31)

where the inequality is by Lemma
C.15
.
If
\max\{\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)},\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}\}=O(1)
, we have the following bound

\displaystyle F_{j}(\mathbf{W}_{j}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r=1}^{m}[\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},j\cdot\bm{\mu})+\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle)]

\displaystyle\leq 2\cdot 3^{q}\max_{j,r,i}\bigg\{\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)},\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)},32nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot\log(T^{\ast})\bigg\}^{q}

\displaystyle=O(1),

where the first inequality is by (
C.30
), (
C.31
), and the last line is by
\max_{j,r,i}\{\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)},\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}\}=O(1)
and Condition
4.1
which implies
128nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot\log(T^{\ast})\leq 1
.
∎

Now, we prove the Proposition
C.11
by induction.

Proof of Proposition
C.11
.

By Assumption
C.1
, the results in Proposition
C.11
hold at
t=0
.
Suppose that there exists
\widetilde{T}\leq T^{*}
such that the results in Proposition
C.11
hold for all time
0\leq t\leq\widetilde{T}-1
,
we aim to prove Proposition
C.11
also hold for
t=\widetilde{T}
.

1.

Proof of (
C.18
) holds for
t=\widetilde{T}
, i.e.,
\underline{\rho}^{(t)}_{j,r,i}\geq-64nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot\log(T^{\ast})
for
t=\widetilde{T}
,
r\in[m]
,
j\in\{\pm 1\}
and
i\in[n]
:

Notice that
\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}=0
, for
j=y_{i}
. Therefore, we only need to consider the case that
j\not=y_{i}
.
When
-64nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot\log(T^{\ast})\leq\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(\widetilde{T}-1)}\leq-32nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot\log(T^{\ast})
, by Lemma
C.15
we have that

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(\widetilde{T}-1)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\leq\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(\widetilde{T}-1)}+32nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot\log(T^{\ast})\leq 0,

and thus by (
C.7
),

\displaystyle\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(\widetilde{T})}

\displaystyle=\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(\widetilde{T}-1)}+\frac{\eta}{nm}\cdot\ell_{i}^{\prime(\widetilde{T}-1)}\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(\widetilde{T}-1)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle)\cdot\ind(y_{i}=-j)\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(\widetilde{T}-1)}

\displaystyle\geq-64nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot\log(T^{\ast}),

where the last inequality is by induction hypothesis.
When
-64nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot\log(T^{\ast})\leq-32nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot\log(T^{\ast})\leq\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(\widetilde{T}-1)}\leq 0
,
by Lemma
C.15
we have that

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(\widetilde{T}-1)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle\leq\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(\widetilde{T}-1)}+32nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot\log(T^{\ast})\leq 32nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot\log(T^{\ast}),

(C.32)

thus we have that

\displaystyle\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(\widetilde{T})}

\displaystyle=\underline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(\widetilde{T}-1)}+\frac{\eta}{nm}\cdot\ell_{i}^{\prime(\widetilde{T}-1)}\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(T-1)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle)\cdot\ind(y_{i}=-j)\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq-32nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot\log(T^{\ast})-\frac{\eta}{nm}\frac{3\sigma_{p}^{2}d}{2}\sigma^{\prime}\bigg(32nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot\log(T^{\ast})\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq-32nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot\log(T^{\ast})-\frac{\eta}{nm}\frac{3\sigma_{p}^{2}d}{2}q\bigg(32nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot\log(T^{\ast})\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq-64nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot\log(T^{\ast}),

where we use
\ell_{i}^{\prime(\widetilde{T}-1)}\geq-1
and
\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}\leq\frac{3}{2}\sigma_{p}^{2}d
, and (
C.32
) in the first inequality,
the second inequality is by
32nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot\log(T^{\ast})\leq 1
in Condition
4.1
, and the third inequality is by
\eta=O\big(nm/(q\sigma_{p}^{2}d)\big)
in Condition
4.1
.

2.

The proof of upper bound of
\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}
in (
C.17
) holds for
t=\widetilde{T}
: We have

\displaystyle|\ell_{i}^{\prime(t)}|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1+\exp\{y_{i}\cdot[F_{+1}(\mathbf{W}_{+1}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i})-F_{-1}(\mathbf{W}_{-1}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i})]\}}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\{-y_{i}\cdot[F_{+1}(\mathbf{W}_{+1}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i})-F_{-1}(\mathbf{W}_{-1}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i})]\}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\{-F_{y_{i}}(\mathbf{W}_{y_{i}}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i})+\widetilde{C}_{0}\}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\{-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{r^{\prime}=1}^{m}[\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}_{y_{i},r^{\prime}}^{(t)},y_{i}\cdot\bm{\mu})+\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}_{y_{i},r^{\prime}}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle)]+\widetilde{C}_{0})\}.

(C.33)

where the second inequality is due to Lemma
C.9
holds, there exists constant
\widetilde{C}_{0}
such that

F_{j}(\mathbf{W}_{j}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i})\leq\widetilde{C}_{0},j=-y_{i}
,
and the third inequality is by the definition of
F_{y_{i}}
.
Moreover, recall the update rule of
\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)}
and
\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}

in (
C.6
) and (
C.7
),

\displaystyle\gamma_{j,r}^{(t+1)}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)}-\frac{\eta}{nm}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell_{i}^{\prime(t)}\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y_{i}\cdot\bm{\mu}\rangle)\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2},

\displaystyle\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t+1)}

\displaystyle=\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}-\frac{\eta}{nm}\cdot\ell_{i}^{\prime(t)}\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle)\cdot\ind(y_{i}=j)\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}.

Assume there exists
\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}>2m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{\ast})
for some
t\in[0,T^{*}]
, if this does not hold,
\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}\leq 2m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{\ast})\leq 4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{\ast})
holds for all
t\in[0,T^{*}]
, which indicates (
C.17
) holds.
Thus, denote
t_{j,r,i}
to be the last time
t<T^{*}
that
\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}\leq 2m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{\ast})
.
Then we have that

\displaystyle\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(\widetilde{T})}

\displaystyle=\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t_{j,r,i})}-\underbrace{\frac{\eta}{nm}\cdot\ell_{i}^{\prime(t_{j,r,i})}\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t_{j,r,i})},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle)\cdot\ind(y_{i}=j)\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}}_{I_{1}}

\displaystyle\qquad-\underbrace{\sum_{t_{j,r,i}<t<\widetilde{T}}\frac{\eta}{nm}\cdot\ell_{i}^{\prime(t)}\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle)\cdot\ind(y_{i}=j)\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}}_{I_{2}}.

(C.34)

We first bound
I_{1}
as follows,

\displaystyle|I_{1}|\leq

\displaystyle qn^{-1}m^{-1}\eta\bigg(\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t_{j,r,i})}+32nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot\log(T^{\ast})\bigg)^{q-1}\frac{3}{2}\sigma_{p}^{2}d

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle q2^{q}n^{-1}m^{-1}\eta[4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{\ast})]^{q-1}\sigma_{p}^{2}d

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{\ast}),

where the first inequality is by Lemmas
C.8
and
C.15
,
the second inequality is by
32nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot\log(T^{\ast})\leq 2m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{\ast})
and
\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}\leq 2m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{\ast})
,
the last inequality is by
\eta\leq nm/\{6q[4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{\ast})]^{q-2}\sigma_{p}^{2}d\}
in Condition
4.1
.

We then give a bound for
I_{2}
. For
t_{j,r,i}<t<\widetilde{T}
and
y_{i}=j
, we can lower bound
\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle
as follows,

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}-32nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot\log(T^{\ast})

\displaystyle\geq 2m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{\ast})-32nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot\log(T^{\ast})

\displaystyle\geq m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{\ast}),

where the first inequality is by Lemma
C.15
,
the second inequality is by
\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}>2m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{\ast})
due to the definition of
t_{j,r,i}
,
the last inequality is by
32nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot\log(T^{\ast})\leq m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{\ast})
. Similarly, for
t_{j,r,i}<t<\widetilde{T}
and
y_{i}=j
, we can also upper bound
\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle
as follows,

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}+8nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot 4\log(T^{\ast})

\displaystyle\leq 4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{\ast})+32nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot\log(T^{\ast})

\displaystyle\leq 8m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{\ast}),

where the first inequality is by Lemma
C.15
,
the second inequality is by induction hypothesis
\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}\leq 4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{\ast})
,
the last inequality is by
32nm^{\frac{1}{q}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4n^{2}/\delta)}{d}}\cdot\log(T^{\ast})\leq m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{\ast})
. Thus, plugging the upper and lower bounds of
\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle
into
I_{2}
gives

\displaystyle|I_{2}|

\displaystyle=\sum_{t_{j,r,i}<t<\widetilde{T}}\frac{\eta}{nm}\cdot|\ell_{i}^{\prime(t)}|\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle)\cdot\ind(y_{i}=j)\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t_{j,r,i}<t<\widetilde{T}}\frac{\eta}{nm}\cdot\exp(-\frac{1}{m}\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle)+\widetilde{C}_{0})\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle)\cdot\ind(y_{i}=j)\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{e^{\widetilde{C}_{0}}\eta T^{*}}{nm}\exp(-\frac{(4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*}))^{q}}{m\cdot 4^{q}})q(4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*}))^{q-1}2^{q-1}\frac{3}{2}\sigma_{p}^{2}d

\displaystyle\leq 0.25T^{*}\exp(-\frac{(4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*}))^{q}}{m\cdot 4^{q}})\cdot 4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*})

\displaystyle=0.25T^{*}\exp(-\log(T^{*})^{q})\cdot 4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*})

\displaystyle\leq m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*}),

where the first inequality is by (
C.33
),
the second inequality is by Lemma
C.8
and upper and lower bound of
\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle
given above,
the third inequality is by
\eta=O\big(nm/\{e^{\widetilde{C}_{0}}q2^{q+2}[4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{\ast})]^{q-2}\sigma_{p}^{2}d\}\big)
in Condition
4.1
, and the last inequality is due to the fact that
\log(T^{*})^{q}\geq\log(T^{*})
.
Plugging the bound of
I_{1},I_{2}
into (
C.34
) completes the proof for
\overline{\rho}_{j,r,i}^{(t)}
.

3.

Similarly, we can prove
\gamma_{j,r}^{(0)}\leq\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)}\leq 4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*})
in (
C.16
).
By
|\gamma_{j,r}^{(0)}|\leq 4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*})
in Assumption
C.1
and
\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)}
is increasing, we have

\gamma_{j,r}^{(0)}\leq\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)}
naturally holds.
Therefore, we only need to prove
\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)}\leq 4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*})
holds for all
0\leq t\leq T^{*}
:
Assume there exists
\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)}>2m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*})
for some
t\in[0,T^{*}]
, if this does not hold,
\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)}\leq 2m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*})\leq 4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*})
holds for all
t\in[0,T^{*}]
indicates (
C.16
) holds.
Thus, denote
\widetilde{t}_{j,r}
to be the last time
t<T^{*}
that
\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)}\leq 2m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*})
hold.
Then we have that

\displaystyle\gamma_{j,r}^{(\widetilde{T})}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{j,r}^{(\widetilde{t}_{j,r})}-\underbrace{\frac{\eta}{nm}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell_{i}^{\prime(\widetilde{t}_{j,r})}\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(\widetilde{t}_{j,r})},y_{i}\bm{\mu}\rangle)\cdot\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}}_{I^{\prime}_{1}}

\displaystyle\qquad-\underbrace{\sum_{\widetilde{t}_{j,r}<t<\widetilde{T}}\frac{\eta}{nm}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell_{i}^{\prime(t)}\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y_{i}\bm{\mu}\rangle)\cdot\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}}_{I^{\prime}_{2}}.

(C.35)

We first bound
I^{\prime}_{1}
as follows,

\displaystyle|I^{\prime}_{1}|\leq\eta n^{-1}m^{-1}nq\left(\gamma_{j,r}^{(\widetilde{t}_{j,r})}\right)^{q-1}\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}\leq qm^{-1}\eta(2m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*}))^{q-1}\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}\leq m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*}),

where the first inequality is by Lemma
C.9
and
C.10
,
the second inequality is by
\gamma_{j,r}^{(\widetilde{t}_{j,r})}\leq 2m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*})
,
the last inequality is by
\eta\leq m\cdot 2^{q-3}/\{q[4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*})]^{q-2}\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}\}
in Condition
4.1
.

We then bound
I^{\prime}_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle|I^{\prime}_{2}|=

\displaystyle\sum_{t_{j,r,i}<t<\widetilde{T}}\frac{\eta}{nm}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\ell_{i}^{\prime(t)}|\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y_{i}\bm{\mu}\rangle)\cdot\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{t_{j,r,i}<t<\widetilde{T}}\frac{\eta}{nm}\cdot\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\ell_{i}^{\prime(t)}|\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y_{i}\bm{\mu}\rangle)\cdot\ind(y_{i}=j)\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\ell_{i}^{\prime(t)}|\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y_{i}\bm{\mu}\rangle)\cdot\ind(y_{i}\neq j)\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{t_{j,r,i}<t<\widetilde{T}}\frac{\eta}{nm}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\ell_{i}^{\prime(t)}|\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y_{i}\bm{\mu}\rangle)\cdot\ind(y_{i}=j)\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}

(C.36)

where the third equality is by
\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y_{i}\bm{\mu}\rangle=-\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)}\leq 0
in Lemma
C.9
.
For
t_{j,r,i}<t<\widetilde{T}
, we upper bound
\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y_{i}\bm{\mu}\rangle,j=y_{i}
(namely
\langle\mathbf{w}_{y_{i},r}^{(t)},y_{i}\bm{\mu}\rangle
) as

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y_{i}\bm{\mu}\rangle=y_{i}\cdot j\cdot\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)}=\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)}\leq 4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*})

where the equality is by Lemma
C.10
,
the second inequality is by induction hypothesis
|\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)}|\leq 4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*})
.
For
\widetilde{t}_{j,r}<t<\widetilde{T}
and
y_{i}=j
, we can also lower bound
\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y_{i}\bm{\mu}\rangle
(namely
\langle\mathbf{w}_{y_{i},r}^{(t)},y_{i}\bm{\mu}\rangle
) as follows,

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y_{i}\bm{\mu}\rangle=y_{i}\cdot j\cdot\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)}=\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)}\geq 2m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*})

where the inequality by
\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)}>2m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*})
due to the definition of
\widetilde{t}_{j,r}
.
Thus, plugging the upper bound of
\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y_{i}\bm{\mu}\rangle
and the lower bound of
\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y_{i}\bm{\mu}\rangle
when
y_{i}=j
into
|I^{\prime}_{2}|
(
C.36
) gives

\displaystyle|I^{\prime}_{2}|

\displaystyle=\sum_{t_{j,r,i}<t<\widetilde{T}}\frac{\eta}{nm}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\ell_{i}^{\prime(t)}|\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y_{i}\bm{\mu}\rangle)\cdot\ind(y_{i}=j)\cdot\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t_{j,r,i}<t<\widetilde{T}}\frac{\eta}{nm}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{n}\exp\big(-\frac{1}{m}\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y_{i}\bm{\mu}\rangle)+\widetilde{C}_{0}\big)\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y_{i}\bm{\mu}\rangle)\cdot\ind(y_{i}=j)\cdot\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{e^{\widetilde{C}_{0}}\eta T^{*}}{m}\exp\bigg(-\frac{(4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*}))^{q}}{m\cdot 2^{q}}\bigg)q(4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*}))^{q-1}\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 0.25T^{*}\exp\bigg(-\frac{(4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*}))^{q}}{m\cdot 2^{q}}\bigg)\cdot 4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*})

\displaystyle\leq 0.25T^{*}\exp\big(-\log(T^{*})^{q}\big)\cdot 4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*})

\displaystyle\leq m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*}),

where the first inequality is by (
C.33
),
the second inequality is by the upper bound of
\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y_{i}\bm{\mu}\rangle
and the lower bound of
\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y_{i}\bm{\mu}\rangle
(
y_{i}=j
) given above,
the third inequality is by
\eta\leq O\big(m/\{4e^{\widetilde{C}_{0}}q[4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*})]^{q-2}\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}\}\big)
in Condition
4.1
, and the last inequality is due to the fact that
\log(T^{*})^{q}\geq\log(T^{*})
.
Plugging the bound of
I^{\prime}_{1},I^{\prime}_{2}
into (
C.35
) completes the proof for
\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)}
.

Therefore, Proposition
C.11
holds for
t=\widetilde{T}
, which completes the induction.
∎

Finally, the following Lemma
C.12
, which is based on Proposition
C.11
, is proved.

Proof of Lemma
C.12
.

Firstly, we prove that

\displaystyle-\ell^{\prime}\big(y_{i}f(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i}))\cdot\|\nabla f(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i}\big)\|_{F}^{2}=O(\max\{\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2},\sigma_{p}^{2}d\}).

(C.37)

Without loss of generality,
we suppose that
y_{i}=1
and
\mathbf{x}_{i}=[\bm{\mu}^{\top},\bm{\xi}_{i}]
. Then we have that

\displaystyle\|\nabla f(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i})\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j,r}\bigg\|\big[\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\mu}\rangle)\bm{\mu}+\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle)\bm{\xi}_{i}\big]\bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j,r}\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\mu}\rangle)\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j,r}\sigma^{\prime}(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},\bm{\xi}_{i}\rangle)\|\bm{\xi}_{i}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2q\bigg[F_{+1}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{+1},\mathbf{x}_{i})\bigg]^{(q-1)/q}\max\{\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2},2\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d}\}

\displaystyle\qquad+2q\bigg[F_{-1}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{-1},\mathbf{x}_{i})\bigg]^{(q-1)/q}\max\{\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2},2\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d}\}

\displaystyle\leq 2q\Bigg\{\bigg[F_{+1}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{+1},\mathbf{x}_{i})\bigg]^{(q-1)/q}+\big[1+4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*})\big]^{(q-1)/q}\Bigg\}\max\{\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2},2\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d}\},

where the first and second inequalities are by triangle inequality, the third inequality is by Jensen’s inequality and Lemma
C.8
. The last inequality is by Lemma
C.9
, i.e.,

\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=-y,r\in[m]}\sigma(\langle\mathbf{w}_{j,r}^{(t)},y\bm{\mu}\rangle)\leq\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=-y,r\in[m]}\sigma(-\gamma_{j,r}^{(t)})\leq\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=-y,r\in[m]}\sigma(-\gamma_{j,r}^{(0)})\leq\max_{j,r}\{0,(-\gamma^{(0)}_{j,r})^{q}\}\ll 4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*})
,
therefore,
F_{-1}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{-1},\mathbf{x}_{i})\leq 1+4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*})
by Lemma
C.9
.
Denote
A=F_{+1}(\mathbf{W}_{+1}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i})
, then we have
A\geq 0
.
Thus,

\displaystyle-\ell^{\prime}\big(y_{i}f(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i})\big)\cdot\|\nabla f(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i})\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq-\ell^{\prime}(A-1-4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*}))\cdot 4q^{2}\bigg\{A^{(q-1)/q}+\big[1+4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*})\big]^{(q-1)/q}\bigg\}^{2}\cdot\max\{\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2},2\sigma_{p}\sqrt{d}\}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad=-4q^{2}\ell^{\prime}(A-1-4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*}))\bigg\{A^{(q-1)/q}+\big[1+4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*})\big]^{(q-1)/q}\bigg\}^{2}\cdot\max\{\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2},4\sigma_{p}^{2}d\}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\bigg\{\max_{z>0}-4q^{2}\ell^{\prime}(z-1-4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*}))\{z^{(q-1)/q}+\big[1+4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*})\big]^{(q-1)/q}\}^{2}\bigg\}\cdot\max\{\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2},4\sigma_{p}^{2}d\}

\displaystyle\qquad\overset{(i)}{=}O(\max\{\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2},\sigma_{p}^{2}d\}),

where (i) is by
\max_{z\geq 0}-4q^{2}\ell^{\prime}(z-1-4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*}))(z^{(q-1)/q}+\big[1+4m^{\frac{1}{q}}\log(T^{*})\big]^{(q-1)/q})^{2}<\infty
because
\ell^{\prime}
has an exponentially decaying tail. Now we can upper bound the gradient norm
\|\nabla L_{S}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\|_{F}
as follows,

\displaystyle\|\nabla L_{S}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)})\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell^{\prime}\big(y_{i}f(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i})\big)\|\nabla f(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i})\|_{F}\bigg]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sqrt{-O(\max\{\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2},\sigma_{p}^{2}d\})\ell^{\prime}\big(y_{i}f(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i})\big)}\bigg]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq O(\max\{\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2},\sigma_{p}^{2}d\})\cdot\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}-\ell^{\prime}\big(y_{i}f(\mathbf{W}^{(t)},\mathbf{x}_{i})\big)

\displaystyle\leq O(\max\{\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}^{2},\sigma_{p}^{2}d\})L_{S}(\mathbf{W}^{(t)}),

where the first inequality is by triangle inequality, the second inequality is by (
C.37
), the third inequality is by Cauchy-Schwartz inequality and the last inequality is due to the property of the cross entropy loss
-\ell^{\prime}\leq\ell
.
∎
